### Ratified creative priority (10 Nov 2026): the protagonist is the epic's protagonist
The novel and films are **inspirational reference material**, not the compulsory plot, military chain of command, outcome sequence, character power ranking or protagonist's ceiling. **Historical NPCs are allowed to lose, change factions, fail, survive, die, or be overtaken by the player** when the story supports it. The protagonist may replace famous deeds with their own, rule a faction, overturn a dynasty, or emerge as the age's defining strategist. Do not protect the novel's heroes from justified consequences or make every major event happen without the player's authority. An ordinary/peaceful life remains a heroic story in its own terms.

**Credibility test:** establish player knowledge + travel + existing followers + experience/resources + patron/independent basis + opposition and counteraction + tactical choice + local/campaign effects + downstream faction/court/NPC response. A single choice must not auto-grant an empire; a well-earned chain of choices may. Every chapter should connect at least one **player-caused macro or intimate consequence** to the next. No railroad to a canonical historical victory or to a preset protagonist rank.

### Protagonist-led Chapter Matrix — binding authoring requirements, not proof of full implementation
| Segment | Meaningful protagonist-owned center of action | Legacy/implementation gate |
|---|---|---|
| Prologue (190) | Define convictions, first real action, and own reason to enter the turmoil, not predestined loyalty | Existing introduction; **review later for player-first framing** |
| I (190) | A choice that changes actual companions, camp rights or immediate tactical outcome; early competence with costs | Existing initial choices; **leadership escalation not final** |
| II (190–191) | Lead or reject a dangerous operation on the strength of witnesses' trust, not a historical hero's permission | Existing Chapter II branches; **macro callback to audit** |
| III (191–192) | Direct a detachment/crossing and determine who can survive or claim the road | `ch03_06` narrative agency revised in v0.4.52; downstream political outcome **incomplete** |
| IV (193–195) | Overrule or negotiate a contested route as a legitimate local power, change jurisdiction response | `ch04_04` revised; major land/court response **incomplete** |
| V (196–197) | Issue, enforce or reject a civic settlement with genuine authority and rival pushback | `ch05_04` revised; broader local power consequences **incomplete** |
| VI (198–199) | Independently lead a strategic approach, win/lose objectives, and redirect Xu-area military politics | `ch06_04` revised, 198–199 after-conflict scenes exist; major divergence **not finished** |
| VII (200–207) | Develop a command, political constituency or independent base over years; may rival famous generals | `ch07_04` revised; yearly expansion exists, **large-campaign divergences open** |
| VIII (208–209) | **Red Cliffs**: command an actual fighting force; determine key battle maneuvers, alliances, postwar power and casualties, potentially supplant canonical tactical plans | **3 new player-leadership scenes in v0.4.52**; 9 command/order combinations, 3 settlement stances, IX–XII recollection; **not yet alternate complete battle victory matrix** |
| IX (210–213) | Found/reform territory or negotiate influence; decisions must bind officials and people, not simply win approval | Existing `ch09_04` and `ch09_06` meaningful choices; v0.4.52 war callbacks; **major territorial branches open** |
| X (214–219) | Make or break a strategic pact, campaign, governorship or new power bloc | `ch10_04` revised; Chapter VIII callback added; **full campaign choices open** |
| XI (220–223) | Independently shape dynastic transition, take a crown or build rival legitimacy if earned | `ch11_04` revised; dynastic/sovereign world-state implementation **open** |
| XII (224–234+) | Resolve what the protagonist built, not award whichever ending matches arbitrary scores | `ch12_05` revised; 8 endings draft, **earned gates not verified** |

**Never treat this table as authoring completion or gameplay certification.** For late-story alignment rewrite conflict-era states before claiming radical alternate-history conclusions are implemented. Preserve 12 chapters, 8 endings, 6 romance options and one lifetime.

## WB-02 — TIME, HISTORICAL CAUSALITY AND DISCOVERY RULES

**Historical foundation:** *Romance of the Three Kingdoms* drives story characterisation and dramatic baseline; historical studies cross-check chronology, geography, titles, institutions and actor constraints. Novel dates and historical chronology may diverge; tag both instead of silently harmonising. Fictional roads, councils, meetings and letters are **EDITORIAL_FICTION**, not cited historical occurrences.

| Chapter | Approved production envelope (CE) | Current opening year | What remains to author / check |
|---|---|---|---|
| Prologue / I | 190 | 190 | Player-defined age/origin, genuine initial NPC meetings |
| II | 190–191 | existing local time bridge | Month/day uncertain; no automatic age jump from a single morning |
| III | 191–192 | 191 | Actual routes, crossings and decisions matter |
| IV | 193–195 | 193 | Xu Province resources; Liu Bei/Tao Qian/Mi Zhu encounters must follow actor authority |
| V | 196–197 | 196 | Imperial capital Xu ≠ Xu Province; Liu Bei/Lü Bu/Yuan Shu events must respect political timing |
| VI | 198–199 | **198** | Xiapi context by reports/presence only when location and causality earn it |
| VII | 200–207 | **200** | 201–207 now represented by seven additional scenes; strengthen geography, quality and historical source gates |
| VIII | 208–209 | 208 | Regional alliances, naval logistics, presence and knowledge limits |
| IX | 210–213 | 210 | Western/eastern state and household/economics |
| X | 214–219 | 214 | Multi-year character health, commitments, office, holdings |
| XI | 220–223 | 220 | Rank/survival and succession; no automatic post-223 living cameo |
| XII | 224–234+ | **224** | Late-life chapters and epilogues flexible; death may occur sooner; 234 not mandatory |

**Clock contract:** `storyClock={schema:1, year,month:null,day:null,precision:'year',birthdayKnown:false,ageBasis,anchorScene,legacyYear}`. `novelYear` kept in sync for compatibility. Legacy Chapter VI `novelYear=197` migrates to display 198 and Chapter XII `222` to 224 with *existing numeric age preserved*. Calendar migration is idempotent in tested routes; future chapter jumps affect age approximately, not by fabricated birthdays. Chapter II / period local detail still needs authorial checking.

**Cause-first rule:** record who controls which place, who holds an office, who is alive, how long news travels, which NPC knows it, and which player choices have changed prerequisites. Historical event states: `NOT_YET` / `ONGOING` / `RESOLVED` / `AVERTED` / `ALTERED`; a canonical result must not occur simply because the game has reached a chapter number. Never place an historical NPC bodily in two distant locations; a dated report or courier is an appropriate alternative only when authored and source-plausible. Titles must be contemporaneous, legitimate and never granted prematurely.

**Discovery and personality:** People entries unlock only upon witnessed encounter or legitimate explicit encounter flags, including for imported saves. Motives and personality emerge through observed conduct, not a visible pre-filled profile. Appearance descriptions in prose remain brief when portrait exists; missing portraits do not authorize hallucinated appearances.

**Player-facing anti-spoiler:** Reference cast and ending knowledge here never imply what the protagonist knows. No NPC gains affection, loyalty, marriage, settlement, rank, estate, or a fate by default. Family referred to by relation terms when appropriate (e.g., 'your father'); avoid repetitive exposition.

### v0.4.56 continuity: the four later-Shu figures in 208–209 CE

- `liubei` **Liu Bei:** retains a political cause, independent obligations and the ability to dispute the protagonist; new staged agreement at `ch08_shu56_liubei_compact`, consequences revisited at `ch08_shu56_liubei_settlement`. Not a monarch of Shu Han in this period.
- `zhangfei` **Zhang Fei:** his land rearguard and tactical candor appear in `ch08_shu56_zhangfei_bridge` and `ch08_shu56_zhangfei_shore`; boldness is not stupidity or unearned unconditional loyalty.
- `zhaoyun` **Zhao Yun:** coordinated protection and field command at `ch08_shu56_zhaoyun_road` and `ch08_shu56_zhaoyun_flameline`; his night position is a fictional assigned shoreline detachment with travel and mission continuity, not a miraculous cameo.
- `zhugeliang` **Zhuge Liang:** equal strategic debate, evidence, conditions and skepticism at `ch08_shu56_zhugeliang_terms` and `ch08_shu56_kongming_warroom`; do not present him as the only legitimate historical brain or guarantee his plan succeeds.

These are additions to *existing NPCs*, not four new People entries. Discovery remains inherited from witnessed first meetings. These interactions are **EDITORIAL_FICTION** using known 208 crisis and allied campaign as historical context. Protagonist can surpass all four, serve with them or diverge, within causality; their personalities are revealed through conduct, never a visible secret-traits panel.

**v0.4.51 added (`SOURCE_REVIEWED` actor / `EDITORIAL_FICTION` meeting):** `zhugeliang` Zhuge Liang (Kongming), Liu Bei's adviser by 208 and envoy to Sun Quan; `lusu` Lu Su (Zijing), Sun Quan's representative seeking a military alliance with Liu Bei. Primary anchors: *Sanguozhi* Wu 9 (Lu Su): https://ctext.org/sanguozhi/54 ; *Sanguozhi* Shu 2 (Liu Bei): https://sanguozhi.app/en/chapter/32 ; *Romance of the Three Kingdoms* Chapters 42–43: https://kongming.net/threekingdoms/43 . **Causal & geographic caution:** historical Lu Su met Liu Bei at Dangyang, then Zhuge Liang undertook Jiangdong embassy from Xiakou. The player's invented staff meeting near an unspecified Jiangxia-side refugee river office is **fictional**, not a documented audience or location. No guaranteed knowledge of Sun Quan or Zhou Yu, no Wu command granted, no premature future office or personal friendship. `historical_meet_zhugeliang` on choosing a response after his scene; `historical_meet_lusu` after Lu Su's scene; profile and memories remain unavailable before that. No portrait fabricated. Their independent decisions about diplomacy are not awarded to the player.

**v0.4.55 Shu-circle and Jing new historical actors (`SOURCE_REVIEWED` identities, `EDITORIAL_FICTION` meetings):**
- **Xu Shu (徐庶, Yuanzhi; `xushu`):** adviser associated with Liu Bei and Zhuge Liang before the 208 collapse. Sources record his mother captured during the retreat; he left Liu Bei's service after this crisis. Here the player meets him during a fictional 208 council and responds to his family dilemma. His departure and the mother's plight are not evidence of the novel's forged-letter or suicide episodes; no rescue is automatically won. **Discovery:** `historical_meet_xushu` on exiting `ch08_pb55_xushu`; follow-up choices in `ch08_pb55_xushu_choice`. Chronology/source leads: [Records of the Three Kingdoms — Zhuge Liang biography](https://shijisanguo.com/lang/en/ai/people/xu-shu/), [Kongming Archives Xu Shu](https://kongming.net/encyclopedia/Xu-Shu). Exact dialogue fiction.
- **Liu Qi (劉琦; `liuqi`):** elder son of Liu Biao, with Jiangxia troops in 208; NOT a future Shu emperor or automatically a subordinate of the player. Meets the protagonist at the fictional Jiangxia receiving council; controls his own officers and makes a *limited* compact. **Discovery:** `historical_meet_liuqi` on leaving `ch08_pb55_jiangxia`. Historical anchors [Liu Bei biography](https://sanguozhi.app/en/chapter/32), [Rafe de Crespigny chronology, Red Cliffs 208](https://brill.com/previewpdf/book/9789004188303/Bej.9789004185227.i-554_008.xml). The alliance council and protagonist's proposed command are original alternative-history fiction.
- **Existing future Shu-associated cast:** Liu Bei, Guan Yu, Zhang Fei, Zhao Yun, Zhuge Liang, Mi Zhu, Sun Qian, Jian Yong appear or are referenced in prelude scenes according to 208 plausibility; specific witnessed flags set when interactions occur (Zhao Yun at the column, the other staff at the command council). Do not claim the state of **Shu Han** already exists in 208 CE (it is not yet founded). No arbitrary new portrait keys. Historical achievements of Zhao Yun, Zhang Fei and Guan Yu remain significant but cannot veto earned player actions. Other roles may diverge once causal prerequisites change.

**v0.4.71 historical continuity note (EDITORIAL_FICTION / chronology-constrained):** The 232 audience is with customs officers and envoys, **not** simultaneous in-person audiences with three rival sovereigns. The public names of Liu Shan, Sun Quan and Cao Rui reflect baseline political claims only where a divergent game route has not invalidated them. Conditional reports can reflect previously witnessed Zhuge Liang, Lu Xun, Liu Bei and Zhao Yun scenes, but never imply the latter two are physically present or survive to 234. These names are not newly registered People. Historical reports are limited to information a messenger could carry; military commands and troop rights are not automatically granted by fame. Source-level event precision, divergent survival and exact offices remain subject to historical editorial review.

### Source-review notes for implemented historical additions

- **Gongsun Zan** — actual authored coalition liaison to Liu Bei in Ch I `ending`; literary plausibility linked to novel Ch 5, but the particular report and his dialogue are fictional. `historical_meet_gongsunzan` is set only through legitimate action leaving the scene. Cao Cao and Yuan Shao mentioned at this stage are indirect staff/dispatch names, **not personal encounters**.
- **Zhao Yun** — Ch III portrayal belongs to source-dependent novel/historical geography and Liu Bei/Gongsun Zan association; preserve his original chronology and verify exact direct encounter in HTML before extending.
- **Mi Zhu** (`mizhu`, Zizhong) — Donghai wealthy merchant family; Tao Qian's Xu Province financial/logistical assistant before Tao Qian's death in 194; Ch IV invented council/road interview is fictional. First witnessed Ch IV `ch04_02`; Xu Province visitor cannot command every unrelated bridge or road. Later service to Liu Bei not granted ahead of story time.
- **Sun Qian** (`sunqian`, Gongyou) — from Beihai, associated with Liu Bei's staff and later diplomacy. Fictional evidentiary/logistics meeting in `ch05_02`, discovery on leaving through a real choice. `ch05_05` winter-policy responses and `ch06_01`/`ch07_01` conditional notes; no automatic rank/friendship or in-person teleportation.
- **Jian Yong** (`jianyong`, Xianhe) — Liu Bei's old companion, irreverent tone; appears in Ch V `ch05_04` council, discovery on the original winter-policy choice. His particular jokes/advice are authored fiction. Later callbacks may be work recollections, not physical Ch VII cameo. Do not pre-grant later title.
- **Guan Yu Ch VII** — `ch07_06` formerly implied physical presence during uncertain 200 CE separation; v0.4.44 instead has an earlier inspected record and fictional local captain. The correction is editorial fiction, not a canonical historical audience.

**Useful sources recorded by v1.35:** *Romance of the Three Kingdoms* chapters 5 and 14–27 (https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/Translation:Romance_of_the_Three_Kingdoms/Chapter_5 ; https://www.chinese-novels.com/book/the-romance-of-the-three-kingdoms/); *Sanguozhi* Shu 8 biographical translations (https://sanguozhi.app/en/chapter/38); chronological cross-check (https://kongming.net/novel/chronology/). These are **source leads**, not global verification of each fictional meeting.

**Portrait inventory actually documented:** `liubei`, `guanyu`, `zhangfei`, `shen`, `hansheng`, `xuwen`, `qinrui`, `dengyu` (embedded WebP asset keys). Additional People IDs may lack bespoke portrait. A key being registered is not proof of editorial portrait acceptance. Maintain stable ID, discovery gating, offline HTML embedding, visual continuity.

**Historical casting capacity:** 100–120 distinct figures (planning allocation ~24 major, 40 supporting, 48 background); NOT an already-researched cast. Repeat names count once. Tag `HISTORICAL_NOVEL`, `NOVEL_FICTION` (e.g., Diao Chan), `ORIGINAL`, `SOURCE_REVIEWED`, `CANON_VERIFIED` or `EDITORIAL_FICTION` precisely; never claim novel fiction is historical biography.

**v0.4.72 direct historical witness amendment (208 CE, EDITORIAL_FICTION):** Guan Yu's Hanjin command and existing ferry presence are inherited from the v0.4.55 staged crossing; his new `ch08_c72_01_guanyu_council` encounter is bodily witnessed and can unlock `guanyu` People discovery, even without the original first-act introductory scenes. Lu Su appears only at the earlier authenticated `ch08_lusu_quay` river-office scene, then again at the same lower quay in the immediately linked `ch08_c72_03_lusu_table` / `04_eastern_conditions`. He is a messenger to Sun Quan, not himself sovereign. Both have independent goals, and later memory in 210+ uses paper witnesses/couriers rather than invented cross-China travel. No new historical dossiers or portraits are created; historical anchors remain Guan Yu at Hanjin and Lu Su's eastern alliance diplomacy. Authored specific conversation/dialogue is fiction, not a primary-source transcript.

## WB-04 — SIX ROMANCE ROUTES, FRIENDSHIP AND INDEPENDENT LIVES

**v0.4.60 — 222 CE personal gathering (IMPLEMENTED, limited validation):** A legitimate, independently attended neutral river exchange offers **at most one** job-specific face-to-face meeting for already eligible adult Shen Lian, Lin Yue, He Qing, Cai Zhen, Lan, or Song Wan. Each meeting is distinctive and supports freely chosen affection, friendship, or parting, without automatic marriage, cohabitation, exclusive ownership, status or a seventh romance. If the player explicitly chose distance in the earlier 217 meeting, it is not reopened. A 217 friendship cannot automatically become a romantic invitation. Family-court consent and escort constraints remain active for Cai Zhen. Those who never met a candidate do not gain a new letter or romance; the solo path skips all private meetings. The 223 narrative remembers only the chosen meeting and does not invent a shared household. **Remaining open:** lifetime intimacy and cohabitation are still only partial; six complete routes and conditional ending treatment remain unfinished.


**LATEST USER CREATIVE RULING (11 November 2026; ACTIVE):** Direct conversations and meetings are the preferred vehicle of romance. Letters are brief practical connectors when distance makes meetings impossible, never a recurring substitute for chemistry. v0.4.59 implemented one optional job-grounded face-to-face 217 meeting for each of six previously-known eligible women with a return in 218; the player meets **one per gathering**, not all six simultaneously. Shen/river pilot Lin Yue/healer He Qing/minister's daughter Cai Zhen/performer Lan/trader Song Wan remain independent adults. Respect conditions, consent, distinct voices, credible travel and career obligations; never rewrite historical couriers as personal meetings. Earlier v0.4.58 letter path is **only a mid-save compatibility branch**, not current default; future stories should favor earned reunions. This is a design and partial implementation milestone, **not completion of six lifetime romance stories**.


**One shared life; not a dating simulator:** S0 Shen Lian **plus FIVE OTHER** adult women R1–R5, all as possible substantial arcs. These are six distinct optional opportunities, NOT six fully implemented romances or six guaranteed unions. All require adult participants, reciprocal consent, independent goals, age and chronology plausibility. Refusal, platonic bonds, solitude, commitment and mutually negotiated plural households must each retain plausible consequences. No graphic sexual acts. Entertainment venue participation never assumes romantic/sexual availability.

R2 He Qing: `ch09_01` (210 CE) → `ch09_heqing_clinic` → optional `ch09_heqing_lantern` → `ch09_02`, only after genuine `romance_meet_heqing`, initial `open`, second `open` and adult status. He Qing independently reaches the post-208 relief market by a two-year shelter network relocation, not via magical delivery of the 198 medical packet. R5 Song Wan: the adult player's actual warm reply to her 214 `ch10_02` second-choice invitation (requires discovered first `open` stance) → `ch10_songwan_accounts` → optional `ch10_songwan_lamplight` → original `ch10_03`. Her existing warehouse inspection and independent commercial convoy make a local meal possible; no property or marriage is granted. R3 Cai Zhen: `ch11_02` (220 CE) → `ch11_cai_waystation` → optional `ch11_cai_lamplight` → `ch11_03` only for actual prior meet, initial and second `open`, discovered court-family evidence `cai_court_known`, qualifying `investigate|protect` choice and player `cai_future_stance='seek'` from 210. The intervening decade is respected and her party travels under a fictional licensed civilian petition arrangement after changes to northern court authority. **Historical office/neutral-corridor specifics remain `EDITORIAL_FICTION / TO_VERIFY`, not verified historical incidents.** Each choice can refuse intimacy, producing a platonic flag; choosing intimacy produces only a private scene and one bounded relationship increment. All participants adult and consenting; night passes non-graphically, professions resume next morning. Observational callbacks at `ch11_01` (He/Song) and `ch12_01` (Cai) occur only after affirmative choices. **No eight-ending solver or existing draft choice group changes**. `isAdult`, discovery and explicit choice guards active. All 6 lifetime romance completions/epilogue QA remain open.

**Flag rules:** encounter IDs `romance_meet_linyue`, `romance_meet_heqing`, `romance_meet_caizhen`, `romance_meet_lan`, `romance_meet_songwan`; initial `romance_stance_*`, second-choice `romance_second_*` values `open|respect|distance`. Only actual choices set values. Forcing a callback in the same scene where a flag is set **upon leaving** is wrong; next-scene or later timestamp is required. The adult `open` option requires an adult character and conditions in source; `distance` and undiscovered paths must not receive uninvited correspondence. Preserve individual memory, delays, travel, and relationships on imported saves. **Always verify names and exact flags in the live HTML when changing handlers.**

**Friendship direction:** 3–4 potential deeply developed platonic lifelong friendships (design target), with authentic service, disagreement, distance, reconciliation, humor, mutual aid and eventual legacy; not automatically linked to romantic score. Companion NPCs can act independently, move, age, change roles or die according to lived events and credible alternative history. Recognition can come through friends and families even without official awards.

**Other adult/social NPCs:** optional venue/supporting female NPCs exist separately from S0–R5 and are not instantly promoted into the six routes. Any expansion uses new stable IDs only when recurring scene work justifies it; age, consent, occupational autonomy, sources and non-graphic writing gates apply.

**Chapter VII v0.4.47 source note:** optional court-family intrigue interlude `ch07_cai_seal` exists after `ch07_04` (200 CE) for legitimately encountered Cai Zhen and `open|respect` secondary stance with non-`distance` initial choice. The main Chapter VII 201–207 progression (`ch07_07`–`ch07_13`) is unchanged. 202/206 payoffs live in existing scene bodies; correspondence is not an in-person meeting or predetermined marriage.

**Chapter VIII–IX v0.4.48 source note (IMPLEMENTED, scoped QA):** `caiLaterEligible` requires actual `romance_meet_caizhen`, non-distance initial stance, second stance `open|respect`, `cai_court_known`, and `cai_court_choice=investigate|protect`. Explicit `leave` or absent contact gets **no new private mail or inserted scenes**. After `ch08_05`, players with valid gates traverse `ch08_cai_accounts` (208 CE) and `ch08_cai_receipt`, preserving `ch08_05` original outcomes. Public/household liability decision `cai_relay_handling` = `verify|shelter|independent`. On later entry from `ch09_02`, only an eligible player who made a relay choice traverses `ch09_cai_threshold` and `ch09_cai_evening` (Chapter IX entry 210 CE), setting `cai_future_stance` = `seek|friend|withdraw`. `seek` appears **only** after a true `open` second stance and is an invitation with refusal rights, not a secret guaranteed love ending. `respect` may choose friendship or withdrawal. `ch10_01` (214 CE) adds a delayed, stance-specific note; personal travel, court office, relationship and marriage are **not** manufactured. New scene years follow the existing chapter clock; no exact dates of birth are inferred. Original People IDs, strategic choice definitions, ending blocks and save key unchanged. These are four additional scenes, not four new romantic options or completed lifetime routes.


## WB-05 — NOVEL BLUEPRINT / CHAPTER CARD AUTHORITY

**Chapter XII implementation card v0.4.62 (225–234 CE):** The protagonist decides what institutions or compacts to preserve in 225, selects a mission in 227, exercises direct or delegated military command OR negotiation/civilian protection in 228, pays the real cost in 229, chooses one optional legitimate in-person reunion during 230, and makes a practical governance decision in 232 before 234 and the original lifetime resolution. Historical northern campaigns (Zhuge Liang, Wei resistance) are baseline context only; no forced encounter, canonical outcome, historical death or secret gate leaks. Conditional Zhuge Liang consultation exists only after actual prior meeting and western political alignment with travel explained. **PARTIAL** — 8 ending manuscripts still require earned-feasibility arbitration and full testing.


**Chapter XI v0.4.61 extension (222 CE):** Any commander with the appropriate raid opportunity may personally fight at the ford regardless of charter/western/civic political status. Three battlefield stances and two tactical consequences make frontal combat optional, costly and narratively recognized; the opposing captain is a fictional battlefield actor, **not** an automatic historical-NPC duel. The original raid/parley/shelter choice effects still apply, with a revised aftermath to avoid repeating the action and an echo in Chapter XII. This should be expanded to appropriately earned ruler/combat choices in earlier and later chapters without universal invulnerability or mandatory personal combat.

**Chapter XI new implementation card v0.4.60 (220–223):** The protagonist hears Cao Pi's Wei mandate (220), faces rival claims and Liu Bei's western Han succession (221), chooses a personal response to the eastern campaign in which Lu Xun's military capability matters (222), and enters a conditional political settlement amid western succession after Liu Bei's death (223). Cao Pi and Liu Bei are historical actors beyond the protagonist's immediate reach, represented through believable authenticated relays; Zhuge Liang and Zhao Yun influence the administrative and military debate without teleporting to the river. Lu Xun can be confronted at a fictional guarded sandbank parley only if explicitly selected. The protagonist can lead armed action, broker limits, defend civilian supplies and contest political authority; local institutions may obstruct or disagree. Every big decision has a 223 account and Chapter XII echo. **Not** a complete province-level state machine or dynamically rewritten timeline if player previously conquered all of China. Historical baseline research anchors: Sanguozhi chronology (220 Wei, 221 Han, 222 Yiling, 223 western succession); see https://shijisanguo.com/lang/en/ and Rafe de Crespigny, *Generals of the South*, ANU repository. Do not equate historically attested events with the original protagonist's fictional meetings or outcomes.


**Status:** Working **design blueprint**, NOT confirmation every chapter is completely authored, historically verified or gameplay-tested. Four Acts / twelve chapters remain the protected scope; four service lanes share a single world. Expand local prose by scene batch and carry material choices into later consequences. Source exact chapter-card texts below are retained from v1.35; do not treat draft date references as proof of live gameplay timing.

### B0. Design invariants and no-spoiler authoring boundary
1. **Novel first:** every chapter delivers scene-length prose with distinctive voice, dramatic escalation, humane aftermath, and no repetitive recap or game-mechanical exposition. Not every chapter requires the five literary pillars in equal proportions; across adjacent chapters no pillar may become permanently absent.
2. **One life, conditional possibilities:** the protagonist's fixed starting age of sixteen, unknown exact birthday and life events evolve by elapsed fictional time. Early death, retirement, independence, military life, administrative service, love, solitude and family routes remain coherent. A chapter-year anchor is not a guarantee of protagonist presence.
3. **Novel-accurate canon:** the literary novel is primary for recognisable NPC character/power; documentary chronology supports offices, logistics and geography. Baseline world events occur only while preconditions hold; if altered, every downstream memory/location/claim must be recomputed. Never teleport a person or give them future knowledge.
4. **Distinct agency:** each NPC has their own motive, decisions, knowledge and capacity to refuse. Discover identities, portrait, personality and feelings as the protagonist actually learns them. The 5 substantive potential romance routes are campaign-wide and mutually conditional, not a single forced harem; venue women are separate supporting NPCs unless a specific route is deliberately reassigned.
5. **Adult and social realism:** invitations, wine, dance, weddings, brothels/entertainment venues (optional), family gatherings, playful language, household prosperity, consent, tenderness, humor and grief are woven into earned character arcs. Adult sexual intimacy may be acknowledged and sensual **without graphic sexual mechanics**. Shared life is not reduced to a meter or a guaranteed event.
6. **Estate management means people:** cash, grain, land, homes, debt, workshop, gifts and steward decisions may affect tenants, creditors, family and soldiers. Separate `PERSONAL`, `FAMILY`, `TRUSTED/MANAGED`, `OFFICE/FACTION`, `DISPUTED`; never appropriate stores or land simply because of public rank. No estate for characters who never acquire it.
7. **Branch-and-rejoin:** preserve meaningful differences in witnessed choices, NPC reactions, reputations, loss, promises, health and resources at reunions. Do not author 4×12 separate novels; narrative branches converge when geography and politics allow, not automatically in an identical room.
8. **Spoiler protection:** internal names, events, ending IDs and gates are allowed in this document. Keep non-discovered outcomes and NPC identities out of player UI and chat; no public list of hidden romance members.

### B1. Four-act pacing and cross-Act causal ladder
| Act | Window | Long-term change | Dramatic spine | Relationship / lived-in counterpoint | Public consequences that must survive |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| I · I–III | 190–192 | Anonymous outsider → person others can assess | Relief, camp duty, military limits, first choice of loyalties | First friends and possible attraction, chores, ordinary meals and humor | Trust/credibility, first promises, civilian testimony, opening items |
| II · IV–VI | 193–199 | Skilled servant → accountable actor with differentiated duties | Road jurisdiction, tax/grain legitimacy, negotiation versus force | Commitment or refusal, celebrations and ordinary domestic duties if earned | Office access, faction stance, victims/survivors, debt, injury, third-party memories |
| III · VII–IX | 200–213 | Years of work → consequential public/private reputation | Regional wars, migration, river campaign, markets and administration | Relationships across long separations; married, plural, single or solitary lives | Alliance and civilian effects, service lane, project/granary ownership, family continuity |
| IV · X–XII | 214–234+ | Public power or withdrawal → personally earned legacy | War, authority, legitimacy and irreversible choices | Household/children if present, joyful occasions, age, reconciliation, grief | Titles vs actual assets, sovereignty, endings, remembered commitments and deaths |

**Chapter promise:** each chapter needs an experiential opening, 2–4 connected narrative movements, one or more consequential choices, personal aftermath, a credible time/location handoff, and delayed payoff links. Current six short later-draft scenes are scaffolding, not editorial completion. Target 15–20k *authored* words/chapter including variants remains a planning band, never padding.

### B2. Narrative lane architecture — ALL FOUR LANES SHARE ONE WORLD
- `FIELD/COMMAND`: tactical service, soldiers and wounded, military authority earned rather than inherited. A diplomatic or household character can still experience wars indirectly.
- `ADMIN/CIVIC`: records, tax and food, refugee relief, projects, trustworthy civil authority; not a lesser ending simply because no duel occurs.
- `DIPLOMACY/POLITICS`: couriers, alliances, patronage, legitimacy and decisions made under imperfect intelligence.
- `INDEPENDENCE/PRIVATE`: independent local service, property where credibly earned, family, retirement and freedom from rank; this is a valid heroic life, not a failure state.

These are **editorial lane labels, not new user-visible attributes or fixed class selection**. The current `LIFETIME_DRAFT.choices` already uses several tendency keys (`command`, `admin`, `civic`, `politics`, `duty`, `home`, `independence`, `ambition`, `wealth`, `risk`, `retire`). Reuse existing terms only after actual state/serialization inspection; do not invent duplicate meters in production. Lane changes require a narrative bridge, believable appointment and consequences. A character physically in Jingzhou cannot instantaneously attend a northern council.

### B3. Chain of consequences (chapter-level input/output obligations)
| Handoff | Incoming factual state → required authored payoff |
|---|---|
| Prologue → I | Initial name, age, origin responsibility, inventory, personality shown through conduct. |
| I → II | The player was offered/accepted a place in camp; preserve the actual day-after opening of Chapter II and earlier social discovery. |
| II → III | Escort/camp choice and `ch2_35` night → elapsed months and 191 CE bridge; wounds/promises/people survive. |
| III → IV | `ch03_04` civilian ration resolution; `ch03_06` column crossing method → supply trust, route delays, testimony, orders. |
| IV → V | `ch04_04` road outcome + `ch04_06` declared first burden → official trust, civilian safety, administrative mandate. |
| V → VI | `ch05_04` levy decision + `ch05_06` field/district/both → actual supplies, reputational tensions, earned duty. |
| VI → VII | `ch06_04` pass/negotiation/force + `ch06_06` front/rear/liaison → injuries, civic memory, command report, faction contact. |
| VII → VIII | `ch07_04` refugee route + `ch07_06` appointment choice → survivor access, where protagonist serves in 208, who knows. |
| VIII → IX | `ch08_04` river plan + `ch08_06` post-crossing duty → troop losses, boatmen testimony, settlement obligations, alliance trust. |
| IX → X | `ch09_03` relationship boundaries, `ch09_04` works funding, `ch09_06` succession/departure → household choices, debts, built project and political summons. |
| X → XI | `ch10_04` compact + `ch10_06` land/house/service → border position, tenant/family consequences, funds and moral credibility. |
| XI → XII | `ch11_04` legitimacy choice + `ch11_06` settlement/dissent/withdrawal → concrete institutional or independent position, survival and adult family state. |
| XII → Epilogue | `ch12_05` declaration is interpreted alongside the **whole life**. Avoid post-hoc granting authority, romance, possessions or historical survival not earned on route. |

### B4. Historical research reliability register
`NOVEL_EVENT` = novel-focused secondary timeline [Kongming](https://kongming.net/novel/chronology/), corroborated through [MIT hosted academic teaching project](https://romanceofthreekingdoms.pubpub.org/ejt-timeline); `HISTORICAL_CONTEXT` = [Shiji Sanguo historical overview](https://shijisanguo.com/lang/en/) and [Sword & State campaign comparison](https://swordandstate.com/en/history/). These sources establish **broad-year candidate anchors only**. Every scene's exact date, birthplace, name spelling, office, marriage, death, battle tactics and reported speech remains `TO_VERIFY` until checked against relevant novel passages and responsible scholarship. Novel-character canon conflicts are logged rather than silently reconciled. The year windows below are ratified, but the precise suggested scene plan within them is new editorial work.

### B5. Master Chapter Matrix — 13 fully scoped narrative cards
**Per card, ratified year is binding design direction; all plot treatment is AUTHORING_PROPOSAL.** Names in source-observed rows reflect existing text and should not be advertised to players before discovery.

#### Prologue — 190 CE · Act I · *The world breaks before the protagonist understands it*
- **Historical anchor (conditional):** Anti-Dong-Zhuo coalition; imperial crisis and Luoyang upheaval as locally knowable news.
- **Where / who may know:** Chenliu-area vantage; no omniscient view of the capital.
- **Dramatic novel arc:** Establish household/ordinary livelihood and the personal cost of disorder. Allow the player to define ambition through conduct, not a forced personality. Close with a credible reason to leave familiar ground.
- **Real life / social / tonal counterpoint:** A meal, a road rumour, a practical obligation, private family language; one moment of warmth or humor before danger.
- **Conflict:** Who or what deserves protection when regional authority is fragmented?
- **Player agency:** Commit to an initial priority (service, kinship, independence) without permanently locking out later careers.
- **Cast dependency:** People physically present in the opening only; distant warlords remain rumours unless encountered.
- **Romance / independent personal life:** No guaranteed romance/household. Seed one human connection and responsibilities to remember.
- **Resources / property / legitimacy:** Only established starting possessions: spear, robes, 12 coins. No free estate.
- **Exit state and downstream payoff:** Origin obligation, first trust/debt, knowledge of war; protect already-authored setup and selections.
- **Existing-scene provenance:** `opening` (source-observed 190 CE) and actual initialization; chapter blueprint proposed.

#### I — 190 CE · Act I · *A person without title chooses whom to trust*
- **Historical anchor (conditional):** Anti-Dong-Zhuo forces and strained regional command; do not have hero witness every coalition episode.
- **Where / who may know:** Chenliu → first credible camp/district journey; travel and rank need verification.
- **Dramatic novel arc:** A consequential first apprenticeship, encounter with independent people, and a moral choice that earns a believable hearing rather than instant promotion.
- **Real life / social / tonal counterpoint:** Camp meals, strangers sharing shoes or fire, believable friction with senior soldiers, a self-deprecating joke at a quiet moment.
- **Conflict:** The protagonist is skilled but has no authority: obedience, loyalty and civilian need can contradict.
- **Player agency:** Choose how to respond to a duty/mercy dispute while earning limited trust; consequences must echo in II.
- **Cast dependency:** Liu Bei/Guan Yu/Zhang Fei only when actual scene and location permit; early original NPC as already encountered.
- **Romance / independent personal life:** Meaningful first impressions; do not reveal personalities as labels or force affection.
- **Resources / property / legitimacy:** Pay, rations, equipment and any personal expenditure must be small, earned and trackable.
- **Exit state and downstream payoff:** Retain opening choices and discovery flags; Chapter II starts the next day, not months later.
- **Existing-scene provenance:** Existing Chapter I integrated; not re-written or editorially approved by this blueprint.

#### II — 190–191 CE · Act I · *Trust is earned in unglamorous service*
- **Historical anchor (conditional):** Coalition fracture; eastern rivalries spread in 191 (possibly offscreen).
- **Where / who may know:** Locally continuous with the Chapter I camp; chart escort routes and return without teleportation.
- **Dramatic novel arc:** Build relationships and personal reputation through escorting, camp decisions and consequences. Show at least one costly but unspectacular act of responsibility.
- **Real life / social / tonal counterpoint:** Watches, cold food, tents, medical care, teasing and conversation; first awareness that a friendship can become personally important.
- **Conflict:** The group depends on a poorly resourced chain of orders and partial information.
- **Player agency:** Honor or contest a promise, respond to endangered people, and settle a trust conflict; respect existing early choices.
- **Cast dependency:** Existing registered original companions, documented senior officers when physically present. No surprise 120-person roster.
- **Romance / independent personal life:** Preserve documented approach / trust / rejection; no new marriage or romantic entitlement.
- **Resources / property / legitimacy:** Escort supplies belong to camp; do not turn government stores into personal assets.
- **Exit state and downstream payoff:** `ch2_35` closes a camp night; `ch03_01` now establishes elapsed months to early 191.
- **Existing-scene provenance:** `ch2_01`, `ch2_35`, `ch03_01` handoff; DATE_DETAIL_OPEN.

#### III — 191–192 CE · Act I · *Responsibility beyond the campfire*
- **Historical anchor (conditional):** Yuan Shao–Gongsun Zan contest; 192 Dong Zhuo downfall as believable distant news; not necessarily direct participation.
- **Where / who may know:** Travel and crossing at a ford; identify starting district before rewriting `ch03_06` orders.
- **Dramatic novel arc:** Supply and civilian disputes test whether a highly able but low-ranking protagonist can exercise judgement with witnesses. A final movement order expands the world.
- **Real life / social / tonal counterpoint:** An unnamed civilian family asking for aid, an awkward quartermaster disagreement, group training, private camp conversation; allow warmth and gentle humor.
- **Conflict:** Emergency food distribution versus written duty; safety of carts versus speed.
- **Player agency:** `ch03_04` ration aid: care/share/shelter; `ch03_06` crossing: slow/ahead/route. Give each trade-off and future memory.
- **Cast dependency:** Source-observed: Han Sheng, Shen Lian, Xu Wen, Guan Yu, Liu Bei; other characters may only be introduced through verified scenes.
- **Romance / independent personal life:** Strengthen trust, friendship or romantic possibility conditionally; do not cast unnamed woman automatically as romance.
- **Resources / property / legitimacy:** Camp ration is state property; preserve issue ledger and witness, not personal coin gain.
- **Exit state and downstream payoff:** Store civilian welfare and military reliability; IV road/jurisdiction crisis should notice crossing outcomes.
- **Existing-scene provenance:** `ch03_01`–`ch03_06`; 2 authored decision groups.

#### IV — 193–195 CE · Act II · *The road is an instrument of power*
- **Historical anchor (conditional):** Xu Province crisis (193–194), contest for Yan, Liu Bei in shifting regional roles, imperial flight in 195.
- **Where / who may know:** Crossing to road district must have dated journey from 192; no assertion that every route reaches Xu.
- **Dramatic novel arc:** A blocked eastern road becomes a political test: law and armed power are neither identical nor simple. Show refugees and residents with their own motives.
- **Real life / social / tonal counterpoint:** Rain beneath leaking eaves, a ledger and a private conversation, roadside workers, celebration after a lawful compromise (or a somber meal after loss).
- **Conflict:** Territory access, civilian movement and command credibility.
- **Player agency:** `ch04_04` letter/move/both; `ch04_06` people/ranks/freedom. These begin differentiated career emphasis without locking lanes.
- **Cast dependency:** Early circle if present; regional officials and local commander need new ID only if recurring. Source-name candidates require scene windows.
- **Romance / independent personal life:** First candid disagreements and reconciliations; affection must survive different views on risk.
- **Resources / property / legitimacy:** Road toll, provision inventories, written authorizations; no private extraction of public revenue.
- **Exit state and downstream payoff:** Maintain civilian and officer evaluations separately; V uses the legitimacy of IV actions.
- **Existing-scene provenance:** `ch04_01`–`ch04_06`; 2 choice groups, draft text only.

#### V — 196–197 CE · Act II · *The pen can injure more than the spear*
- **Historical anchor (conditional):** Cao Cao brings emperor to Xu (196); Yuan Shu imperial claim and Wancheng crisis (197) as conditional anchors.
- **Where / who may know:** District office or garrison; Xu court may be reported indirectly from other jurisdictions.
- **Dramatic novel arc:** Grain and tax records reveal real households. A winter levy fight forces the protagonist to choose between relief, investment and institutional defensibility.
- **Real life / social / tonal counterpoint:** A dispute by a mill, clerks handling registers, winter food, a council, an intimate conversation or modest feast away from official rooms.
- **Conflict:** Legitimacy versus immediate welfare; administrative corruption and uneven harvest impacts.
- **Player agency:** `ch05_04` relief/work/audit; `ch05_06` field/district/both. Record actual cost and who pays it.
- **Cast dependency:** Existing Shen Lian and other named companions conditional on routes; court names only when news can travel.
- **Romance / independent personal life:** Relationship commitment opportunities where trust is earned; no assumed cohabitation or partner.
- **Resources / property / legitimacy:** Prototype real estate *stakes* through register, rent/tenant grievance, small project or debt; no automatic ownership.
- **Exit state and downstream payoff:** Fix witnessed promise and ledger outcome so VI travelling column inherits a real supply position.
- **Existing-scene provenance:** `ch05_01`–`ch05_06`; draft text only.

#### VI — 198–199 CE · Act II · *A passage secured is not a victory without a price*
- **Historical anchor (conditional):** Xiapi campaign across late 198 / novel datings into 199; Yuan Shu collapse and Gongsun Zan downfall (199); should not be forced together on-screen.
- **Where / who may know:** Wells, village and a defended pass; verify who holds road and why the column is there.
- **Dramatic novel arc:** A small engagement or negotiated passage is experienced from the perspective of soldiers and civilians. Another front’s dispatch interrupts apparent closure.
- **Real life / social / tonal counterpoint:** Repairing the village well, wet boots, field surgery, a shared meal after loss, letter or gift from an absent loved one.
- **Conflict:** A vulnerable village holds military leverage; alternatives incur delay, casualties or political obligations.
- **Player agency:** `ch06_04` talk/force/detour; `ch06_06` front/rear/liaison. Deliver survivors, wounds and civic memory to VII.
- **Cast dependency:** Defenders, local residents and tactical commanders; only verified senior novel figures in this geography.
- **Romance / independent personal life:** Distance, pride, missed correspondence and mutually chosen commitments matter more than instant affection.
- **Resources / property / legitimacy:** Military requisitions, injury costs, possible broken trade routes; no estate mandatory.
- **Exit state and downstream payoff:** The corrected year clock enters Chapter VI at 198; the new late-198 to 199 movement preserves prior flags and advances to Chapter VII at 200. Older saves already in Chapter VII or VIII are not retroactively rewound.
- **Implemented source v0.4.46:** Original `ch06_01`–`ch06_06` (two untouched JSON choice groups) plus dynamically registered `ch06_07` (late 198), `ch06_08` (winter 198), `ch06_09` (199), `ch06_10` (199). New choices `ch06_winterDisposition = sealed/relief/escort` at `ch06_08` and `ch06_legacySettlement = office/local/council` at `ch06_10`. The old `ch06_06` choice labels and flags remain, but the destination is now `ch06_07`, delaying year 200 until the new 199→200 exit. Narrative callbacks in `ch07_01`, `ch07_07`, `ch08_04` keep old routes and choices valid.
- **Conditional historical and personal memories:** Mi Zhu, Sun Qian and Jian Yong appear only by delivered paper *if met earlier*; absent lives are not magically physically present. He Qing / Cai Zhen / Lin Yue follow-ups require real prior encounters and qualifying chosen stance; Shen and friends retain independent identities. Xiapi is distant, sourced news rather than an imagined personal attendance.
- **Source check (broad-year anchors, source-dependent):** Rafe de Crespigny, *To Establish Peace* ANU chronology (https://openresearch-repository.anu.edu.au/server/api/core/bitstreams/fcf6cff2-9d85-4b28-add3-f9b4baddc9fd/content), Chapters 61–63; Rafe de Crespigny, *Imperial Warlord* chapter chronology (Brill preview: https://brill.com/previewpdf/book/9789004188303/Bej.9789004185227.i-554_005.xml). Autumn/winter 198 military movement and Xiapi outcome around winter 198 / early 199 are broad historical anchors, not a claim the invented office, bridge, manuscripts or conversation are historical fact. Exact local geography, office appointments and any alternate-history divergence still `TO_VERIFY`.

#### VII — 200–207 CE · Act III · *Years accumulate while the world remakes itself*
- **Historical anchor (conditional):** Guandu (200), Sun Ce/Sun Quan succession (200), Yuan family north consolidation (200–207), Liu Bei in Jingzhou (200s), Zhuge Liang approaches 207/208.
- **Where / who may know:** Multiple districts and orders over years; player can remain in field, court-adjacent work, or local service.
- **Dramatic novel arc:** Four internal movements: 200 major news/pressure; 201–203 displaced lives; 204–206 repeated duty and relationships; 207 renewed strategic horizon. Distinct scenes must justify elapsed years.
- **Real life / social / tonal counterpoint:** An unromantic lodging with a widow, recurring family meal or letters, broken promises remembered, a wedding/festival only where welcome; show people aging.
- **Conflict:** Protection of refugees versus army security versus bargaining with political authorities.
- **Player agency:** `ch07_04` families/army/bargain; `ch07_06` accept/stay/independent; careers receive differentiated duties but converge on major world movement.
- **Cast dependency:** Novel-specific northern/southern factions only where locally encountered or messaged; no instant Zhuge Liang meeting for every branch.
- **Romance / independent personal life:** Long-term romances mature, break, or begin at reasonable windows; children only if causally established, age across skips.
- **Resources / property / legitimacy:** Stewardship of provisions or earned local property may yield a first long-term management decision for qualifying branches.
- **Exit state and downstream payoff:** Retain duty lane, authority, exact place in 207, household/age changes for VIII crisis.
- **Actual code provenance (v0.4.45):** `ch07_01`–`ch07_06` remain the 200 CE opening; `ch07_07` (201), `ch07_08` (202), `ch07_09` (203), `ch07_10` (204), `ch07_11` (205), `ch07_12` (206), `ch07_13` (207) are playable and lead into `ch08_01` (208). Original 2 Chapter VII choices retained, 3 new groups. This is a first extended literary pass, not the 15–20k word target or a claim of six completed romance routes.

**Event and state map (only broad historical anchors, not assumed physical meetings):**
| Scene | Year | News / character events | Choices / lasting state |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ch07_07` | 201 | Guandu aftermath and Liu Bei's movement toward Liu Biao reported by independent carriers; families' transit | `ch07_06` direct original flag preserved; new response `ch07_201_response = families/army/broker` |
| `ch07_08` | 202 | Late confirmation of Yuan Shao death; crop reparations; He Qing update only if met and second stance open/respect | Prior passage choice reaction |
| `ch07_09` | 203 | Winter granary and separate ownership of household vs garrison stores; Cai Zhen enclosure only if eligible | `ch07_winterPolicy = relief/garrison/mixed` |
| `ch07_10` | 204 | Reports of Ye's fall; real winter-cost callback; Lin Yue mark on freight schedule if eligible | Winter callback |
| `ch07_11` | 205 | Northern succession repercussions (Yuan Tan), displaced petition records; optional Lan memory without direct encounter | Conditional costs, friendship |
| `ch07_12` | 206 | Apprentices, succession of duties, choice to decentralize responsibility; optional Cai Zhen letter | `ch07_handover = records/road/people` |

### Long-range consequence and ending contract

### Chapter VIII–IX v0.4.48 targeted insertion (continuity note)
`ch08_05` → optional R3 ledger-account/witness pair → `ch08_06`; `ch09_02` → optional R3 personal-boundary pair → `ch09_03`, then additional delayed memory in `ch10_01`. These conditional private scenes do **not** imply that the protagonist travels to Xu or meets the minister; they occur in the protagonist's current route environment via delayed, dated couriers. The three substantive alternatives each make a public or personal difference, and refusal or no meeting retains the original main path. See WB-04 for exact flags and prerequisites.

### B8. Long-range agency: major player choice clusters and branch convergence
| Cluster | First established / recurrence | Difference that persists after rejoin | Expected late payoff |
|---|---|---|---|
| Duty to civilians vs military order | I–III; VI–VIII | Refugee testimony, trust, supplies, survivor memory | IX economic consent and XI public legitimacy |
| Service lane and faction trust | IV–VII; VIII | Military promotion, records, diplomacy and named witnesses | X authority, XI support/dissent and XII standing |
| Fiscal responsibility and material duty | V, IX–X | Legitimate owner, loans, tenant relief, project performance | XI creditors/dependents; XII stewardship, property or lack thereof |
| Household / romantic commitments | II–IV, VII–X | Individual autonomy, separated letters, existing promises and children if any | XI political/home pressure; XII/E01–E08 personalized outcomes |
| Historical divergence | I–XII whenever causally plausible | Event state, faction leaders' fate, allegiance, geography, successor | Recompute dependent events; no canon outcome silently asserted |
| Health, injury, retirement and mortality | Throughout | Ability limits, caregiving, risk, grave loss, no undo without cause | E08 earlier endings possible; no mandatory 234 survival |

**Choice quality gate:** each authored choice must offer plausible alternatives, foreseeable human stakes, a meaningful near consequence, one delayed payoff or reason not to persist, and a fair outcome based on information and resources. Never add a fake binary choice where both outcomes receive identical prose and relationships. If an outcome cannot be meaningfully remembered, make it a prose-only page.

### B9. Ending architecture / payoff contract — internal IDs not player-facing
**The **current v0.4.44** `LIFETIME_DRAFT.endingBlocks` and `bridges` retain E01–E08 *manuscript/draft material*. The thematic interpretation below is editorially inferred from their existing prose and **must not be mistaken for verified routing/gates**. Do **not** add an ending merely to showcase harem or estate ownership; use conditional prose within eight endings.
| Internal ID | Provisional manuscript focus (verify exact entry conditions) | Essential lifetime dependencies |
|---|---|---|
| E01 | Sovereign/crown and costs of supreme power | Earned authority, legitimacy, governance style, personal relationships and remembered promises |
| E02 | War command under high personal cost | Real military responsibility, survivor/casualty memory, duty versus life, meaningful after-war consequences |
| E03 | Institutional government without sovereignty | Administrative choices, honesty or coercion, citizen welfare and a public succession |
| E04 | Independent territory/mandate | Defended local legitimacy, faction recognition/challenge, households and security |
| E05 | Reformer/builder/civic infrastructure | Repaired communities, debt, named dependents, remembered work and humility |
| E06 | Household/family-centered legacy | Voluntary attachments, estate or lack thereof, family promises, joy, child/kin relationships if earned |
| E07 | Retirement/withdrawn service | What is relinquished, continuing bonds, ordinary survival, memory beyond office |
| E08 | Earlier or later death with dignified closure | Cause, warnings, witnesses, care, promises and survivors; not automatic failure ending |

**Ending arbitration order (DESIGN PROPOSAL; implementation not tested):** (1) a genuinely fatal state with no credible medical escape can end life and select mortality closure; (2) otherwise evaluate earned political/office/service/retirement world position; (3) use earlier choices, relationships and governance for variants; (4) resolve one complete ending and a lifetime epilogue, once. Avoid letting `ch12_05` three labels mechanically generate eight life endings. Where major commitments conflict, author a visible dilemma before transition, not a hidden unfair threshold.

**Branch QA minimum** when HTML changes: one legally navigated save per implemented lane and major outcome, no-romance path, single partner, consented multiple partners if authored, refusal/departure, early mortality and 8 terminal types, plus old-save import migration. Only claim a route tested after actual browser traversal, not a hand-crafted flags object.

## WB-06 — SUPPORTING HUMAN / INSTITUTIONAL SYSTEMS (DESIGN ONLY WHERE NOT IN CODE)

**Purpose:** These are authoring constraints for meaningful service, family, personal life and earned recognition. They are not a mandate to create stat-heavy micromanagement or scenes solely to tick a chapter quota. Confirm an idea is `IMPLEMENTED` in the actual HTML before promising it as gameplay.

### Recognition and titles (period-sensitive)

Earned honors require named witnesses, legitimate authority, dialogue, public/private emotional payoff and later remembered effects. Commendations are not guaranteed rank, military title, promotion or gifts. Official title, courtesy name, nickname, commander role, faction legitimacy, and novel-only Five Tiger honorific are distinct concepts. A fictional *Six Tiger Generals* concept is an **optional alternate-history route**, not historical fact or a built feature; source and justification first.

### Recognition forms and boundaries
| Form | Emotional presentation | Continuity requirement |
|---|---|---|
| Comrades' acknowledgement / public praise | Surprise, soldiers' cheering, friend's jokes, a named witness remembering the deed | What was witnessed and who retells it; no universal acclaim |
| Rescue beneficiaries / intimate gratitude | A parent's relieved thanks, a humble handmade gift, a private friend/family conversation | Recipients' personal stakes and whether gratitude remains private |
| Gift of substance | Carefully described weapon/horse, money, personal keepsake, cloth, residence or other plausible award | Donor, source, ownership, means, obligations and later inventory/estate visibility |
| Military advancement | Summons, experienced officers' testimony, a real increase in command and duty | Rank/title, superior's actual authority, unit, date, reassignment and logistical support |
| Civic / political appointment | Difficult recommendation, legal authority, appointment message or investiture, reactions from peers | Office jurisdiction, eligibility, rival claims, delegated vs personal funds, loss/revocation rules |
| Epithet / sobriquet / alias | Distinctive phrase born naturally of a memorable action, circulated by particular people, sometimes affectionate or ironic | Coiner, exact wording/Hanzi only after language validation, diffusion by location and faction; NOT a rank |
| Court honour or other special title | Proportionate ceremony and extraordinary recognition, not a routine chapter trophy | Verifiable era-valid office/honorific and legitimate/conflicted authority; source check before writing |

### Dramatic scene contract (select scale proportionate to event)
1. **Earned deed:** Show problem, peril, constraints, player choice, NPC aid and cost. Include the real people saved/benefited and whose perspective matters.
2. **Recommendation and suspense:** Make testimony, delayed courier, private patron conversation, political bargaining, uncertainty of being believed and the protagonist's hopes plausible, rather than a surprise grant appearing ex nihilo. A quiet personal thanks needs far less ceremony.
3. **The moment:** Use precise staging—who calls protagonist forward, who grants which thing, who hears it, and why. Speech must name the concrete accomplishment, not generic praise of being 'the chosen one'.
4. **Emotional payoff:** Give the prose space for awe, trembling relief, pride, laughter, tears, friends grinning, partners proud, civilians chanting, mentors withholding then showing admiration, and protagonist response keyed to actual relationships. For some protagonists a quiet earned acknowledgement can beat a lavish banquet.
5. **Celebration and memory:** A meal, reunion, companion toast, gift-giving, intimate family scene, personal visit, new ceremonial responsibility and NPC callbacks. Do not convert every successful scene into immediate punishment; let genuine happiness last.
6. **Later price and opportunities:** New responsibilities and rivals can appear later, but do not erode the emotional victory at the award moment. Tracking later status must be consistent with office, geography, faction, property rights and historical divergence. Never imply 'earned rank' on a route where the player did not achieve the deed or the grantor lacked authority.

### Story arc opportunities (proposals, NOT guaranteed rewards)
- **Act I / Chapters I–III:** Small but precious acknowledgements from comrades/civilians; an early nickname may emerge **only if actually warranted by the witnessed deed**. Initial low standing stays credible; no sudden high court office.
- **Act II / Chapters IV–VI:** Recognition for saving civilian lives, honest records, supply or field performance; realistic rise in entrusted responsibility and optional summons. The recent `ch05_04` policy decision and Chapter VI duties are **possible foundations for later recognition**, not already-awarded office.
- **Act III / Chapters VII–IX:** A truly consequential action can culminate in a substantial command, civic responsibility, diplomatic role, gift or public name; long-distance news and jealous political equals complicate but do not erase joy.
- **Act IV / Chapters X–XII and epilogues:** Distinguish seasoned offices and legitimate grants from affectionate reputation; grand public honour and simple gratitude both can provide a legacy payoff. Friends, partners, children, veterans and communities remember earlier witnessed deeds **when alive, present, informed and related**.

### Data contract (reuse existing state; add flags only at true implementation)
`recognition_id`, `recognition_kind`, `earned_deed_scene_id`, `choice_prerequisites`, `witness_npc_ids`, `evidence_or_report_route`, `delay_and_date`, `nominating_npc_id`, `granting_authority_npc_or_institution`, `verified_period_legal_office_or_TO_VERIFY`, `office_or_title_display`, `epithet_original_and_origin`, `gift_item_or_asset_id`, `ownership_class`, `new_responsibilities`, `public_vs_private_audience`, `recipient_emotional_beat`, `companion_family_response_conditions`, `acceptance_or_refusal`, `faction_and_location_spread`, `later_npc_callbacks`, `promotion_revocation_and_failure_conditions`, `relevant_ending_payoffs`, `scene_ids`, `implementation_status`, `qa_receipt`.

**QA gate:** On implementation, test at least one eligible and ineligible choice history, witness/rumor timing, promotion or refusal outcome, NPC presence/aliveness, property ownership, save import/export, and later callback using developer checkpoints grounded in valid game histories. Distinguish **DESIGNED**, **AUTHORED**, **RUNTIME_VERIFIED** and **EDITORIAL_APPROVED**. Historical titles, ranks, seals, robes, ceremony forms, and nickname language remain `TO_VERIFY` before scene-specific historical claims. Use existing chapter climax/aftermath slots; **no new merit currency, title-grinding UI, separate honours minigame or mandatory promotion quota**.

### Han–Three Kingdoms Titles & Five Tigers Reference Gate v0.1 — APPROVED DIRECTION, SOURCE CHECK PER SCENE (9 Nov 2026)
**Authority:** Master GDD v1.26 §§7A–7C. This section prevents false equivalents between rank, appointment, court honour, nickname and novel-only collective distinction. Listed names are **candidate verified-language leads**, not promotions already written or granted in HTML v0.4.16. Name translations, rank relationships, conferral date and issuing institution remain to be checked before a specific scene is finalized.

| Category | Name used in game / source form | Meaning and limits | Source/verification status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Military, directional | **General Who Campaigns/Conquers the East** · `征東將軍` · *Zhēngdōng Jiāngjūn* | Directional campaign general; not interchangeable with guards/pacifies East | Scholarly rank-family lead; **TO_VERIFY** specific date/realm/grantor |
| Military, directional | **General Who Guards/Pacifies the East** · `鎮東將軍` · *Zhèndōng Jiāngjūn* | Distinct eastern command title, not generic 'General of the East' | Scholarly rank-family lead; **TO_VERIFY** English convention |
| Military, directional | **General Who Pacifies the East** · `平東將軍` · *Píngdōng Jiāngjūn* | Distinct `平東` designation; avoid conflating `鎮` and `平` even if translations overlap | **TO_VERIFY** rank/jurisdiction by regime |
| Military, directional | **General Who Secures the East** · `安東將軍` · *Āndōng Jiāngjūn* | Another distinct eastern title; translate consistently once chosen | **TO_VERIFY** English/rank/date |
| Military, position | `前將軍` Front/Vanguard; `左將軍` Left; `右將軍` Right; `後將軍` Rear | Period-attested position labels, not inherently 'more heroic' than other posts | Source-check actual named appointee and historical window |
| Senior military | `車騎將軍` Chariots-and-Cavalry General; `大將軍` Great General/General-in-Chief | High offices only when real realm, mandate, duties, political preconditions exist | **TO_VERIFY** institutional context |
| Civic government | `太守` Commandery Administrator; `刺史` Inspector | Legal administrative role, location and appointment; not personal land ownership | Han setting attested broadly; **TO_VERIFY** specific scene/form |
| Novel cohort | **Five Tiger Generals** · `五虎將` · *Wǔ Hǔ Jiàng* | Novel's literary grouping of **Guan Yu, Zhang Fei, Zhao Yun, Ma Chao, Huang Zhong**; **not an official historical rank** | **NOVEL_REF** Kongming's Archives; historic contrast in *Records* vol. 36 |
| **Alternate-history novel cohort (game fiction)** | **Six Tiger Generals** · `六虎將` · *Liù Hǔ Jiàng* | **Conditional six-person recognition**: original five + player on a credible later Shu-aligned divergence; not in the real history or the source novel, not a military-grade office | **USER_APPROVED_FICTION**, not a factual source claim; `NOT_IMPLEMENTED` |
| Personal identity | Courtesy name `字` (*zì*), informal sobriquet, popular nickname | Different naming and social functions; nickname can arise organically from a deed, but `zì` is not a military decoration | Each wording, adult naming context, witnesses and diffusion **TO_VERIFY** |
| Court/nobility | Marquis / rank of nobility, honour, posthumous name (distinct) | Check conferral rights, material privileges, date, whether recipient is alive | **TO_VERIFY**; posthumous titles only after death |

**Five/Six Tigers scenario rule:** Under the undiverged novel baseline, the five famous generals remain individually represented as the novel's **Five Tiger Generals**. In an **explicitly divergent player route**, a credible faction leader may proclaim **Six Tiger Generals** (`六虎將`, *Liù Hǔ Jiàng*) with those five plus the protagonist as a new *in-world literary honour*, only if the five members are still available, the player has earned legendary merit, and the authority/timing makes sense. It is **not an office or a historical fact**, not a vacant canonical sixth slot, and not forced on all routes. If the original cohort no longer exists, use a clearly distinct original honour instead, not a falsely canonical six-person group. Do not plant the label or official ceremony too early in the 190s; source-check the relevant narrative-era episode before portraying it. Exceptional player recognition may instead take a distinct military office, an original epithet, a unique gift, or friendship and civil acclaim.

**Scene authoring schema (only when needed):** `appointment_id`, `kind(OFFICIAL_MILITARY|OFFICIAL_CIVIL|NOBILITY|COURT_HONOUR|NOVEL_COHORT|COURTESY_NAME|EPITHET|GIFT)`, `english_display`, `hanzi`, `pinyin`, `source_locator`, `novel_history_difference`, `first_attested_or_target_year`, `valid_authority`, `issuing_realm_and_faction`, `recipient`, `incumbents_and_precedence`, `earned_deed_scene_id`, `testimony_and_transmission`, `appointment_scene_id`, `spoken_formula_or_document`, `command_or_jurisdiction`, `resources_and_personnel`, `player_accept_or_refuse`, `public_awareness`, `relationship_responses`, `subsequent_perks_and_obligations`, `dismissal_or_transfer`, `epilogue_memory`, `implementation_status`, `historical_review_status`, `QA_receipt`.

**Production and emotional gate:** Reward is preceded by an actual deed, demonstrated witness or recommendation, authority deliberation and where appropriate an anticipatory summons. Pause for friends'/family's emotional response and let celebration land; then later show real duties, chain of command, rivals, resources and legal or de facto authority. A civilian achievement is not silently converted to generalship. An unofficial nickname never grants official power. No rank-grinding feature, special office UI or Five Tigers collection mechanics. **Nothing here is already implemented in HTML v0.4.16.**

**Source leads:** Academia Sinica journal (Liao Pak-yuen, 1989, https://toaj.stpi.niar.org.tw/index/journal/volume/article/4b1141f98f86f98c018fa4302df40fa7); historical military-offices thesis (https://central.bac-lac.canada.ca/.item?app=Library&id=TC-OTU-17795&oclc_number=1032896880&op=pdf); Chen Shou, *Records of Three Kingdoms* Shu 36, text/translation requiring cross-check (https://sanguozhi.app/en/chapter/36); Kongming's Archives *Five Tigers* novel encyclopedia (https://kongming.net/encyclopedia/groups/Five_Tiger_Generals_of_Shu). Specific Hanzi, English renderings, dates, individuals and appointment powers must be reviewed against novel passages and primary historical documents as needed.

### Sub-General Officer Posts & Six Tigers Route Ledger v0.1 — USER APPROVED, NOT IMPLEMENTED (9 Nov 2026)
**Authority:** Master GDD v1.26 §7C. These records define candidates and safeguards for **authored future scenes**, not currently reachable promotions, newly registered NPCs or new game engine states. English translations are approximate functional labels rather than modern-equivalent ranks.

#### Below-general military responsibilities — research-backed candidates
Primary institutional basis: *Hou Hanshu* (`百官志一`) organizes a model field force as `校尉` commanding a `部`, `軍司馬` as an officer, `軍候` heading a `曲`, and `屯長` heading a `屯`. The same account documents `別部司馬` for separate contingents and provision for other generals' own formations. De Crespigny explains variation across court, field and frontier armies. **The source does not prove that every warlord army in every chapter followed this exact ladder**.

| office_id / game-facing English | Hanzi / Pinyin | Organizational scope or distinction | Design stage / research status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `MIL_TUNZHANG` / Unit Leader | `屯長` · *Túnzhǎng* | `屯` detachment command; small but real accountability | Candidate early progression; *Hou Hanshu* attested, appointment TO_VERIFY |
| `MIL_JUNHOU` / Company Captain | `軍候` · *Jūnhòu* | `曲` command / `候`; company is a functional translation, not a fixed modern strength | Candidate intermediate command; *Hou Hanshu* attested, scene TO_VERIFY |
| `MIL_JUN_SIMA` / Military Officer | `軍司馬` · *Jūn Sīmǎ* | Officer/major in a `部` structure or command headquarters | Candidate intermediate command; *Hou Hanshu* attested, scene TO_VERIFY |
| `MIL_BIEBU_SIMA` / Independent Detachment Commander | `別部司馬` · *Biébù Sīmǎ* | Separate contingent; troop count varied; not necessarily promoted from `軍司馬` | Conditional appointment; *Hou Hanshu* attested, scene TO_VERIFY |
| `MIL_XIAOWEI` / Colonel | `校尉` · *Xiàowèi* | `部`/regiment or specific court/frontier specialist office | Candidate mid/high command; *Hou Hanshu* attested, scene TO_VERIFY |
| `MIL_DUWEI` / Commandant | `都尉` · *Dūwèi* | Specialized frontier, garrison, cavalry or other post based on **full** title | Context-dependent; de Crespigny research, scene TO_VERIFY |
| `MIL_ZHONGLANGJIANG` / General of the Household | `中郎將` · *Zhōnglángjiàng* | Distinct officer title; could hold field command in the late Han | Context-dependent, **not assumed to be a full general peer**; scene TO_VERIFY |
| `MIL_PIANJIANGJUN` / Junior General | `偏將軍` · *Piānjiāngjūn* | Low-standing **general-titled** appointment, not non-general officer | Separate upper track; historically attested lead, scene TO_VERIFY |
| `MIL_PIJIANGJUN` / Auxiliary General | `裨將軍` · *Píjiāngjūn* | Low-standing **general-titled** appointment, not non-general officer | Separate upper track; historically attested lead, scene TO_VERIFY |

**Narrative progression:** Joining a small detachment, getting entrusted with watch schedules, a first signed order, losing or saving soldiers in a crisis, commanding an independent mission, and receiving a superior's formal recognition are opportunities for distinct earned scenes. Include witnesses, friends' pride, rival reaction, actual men/resources under command and real responsibility. **Not every route must achieve any given office**; political/civil/independent characters retain equal value. Skip irrelevant ranks instead of requiring an RPG checklist. Era-specific unit strengths, organizational labels and chronology still need scene-level fact checks.

#### Optional Six Tiger Generals story arc — fictional, guarded
- `cohort_id`: `ALT_SHU_SIX_TIGERS`; proposed game English `Six Tiger Generals`; proposed Hanzi `六虎將` (*Liù Hǔ Jiàng*); **source classification:** `USER_APPROVED_ALTERNATE_HISTORY_GAME_FICTION`.
- `baseline_cohort`: the **novel** `五虎將` (*Five Tiger Generals*): Guan Yu, Zhang Fei, Zhao Yun, Ma Chao, Huang Zhong. This remains unchanged in no-divergence novel-consistent states. Not a documented Han official military rank.
- `sixth_member`: protagonist **only on a proven eligible route**; do not displace, rename or underpower the five source-novel characters to make room. The roster must actually have six viable living/relevant persons for the specific proposed public ceremony; source novels' later mortality and player history may make this impossible.
- `gates`: `faction_can_confer`; `chronology_allows_cohort`; `five_tigers_canon_status_compatible`; `five_living_or_otherwise_plausibly_recognizable`; `protagonist_major_merits_proven`; `credible_recommendations_and_reports`; `appointment_and_cohort_distinguished`; `player_acceptance`; `public_knowledge_delay`; `future_callbacks`. **No default free title in 190s.**
- `reward`: a major emotional celebration scene—stories of sacrifices recalled, veterans, old friends and loved ones reacting as individuals, praise and tears allowed, eventual duties and rivals. An in-world **cohort honour** is separate from real command, court commission, salary, civic office and property.
- `fallbacks`: if not Shu-aligned, not sufficiently accomplished, rival faction, six-person roster impossible, recognition refused, or the world diverges away from the Five Tigers, retain other proportionate **equally rewarding** military/civil/people's honours; do not leak the unreached label in the UI.
- `implementation_status`: `DESIGN_ONLY`; no new scene, award, flag, rank UI, save migration or browser QA on HTML v0.4.16.

**Internal test gates for future implementation:** Can jump directly to a later existing chapter using the dev QA checkpoint **only for visual/smoke authoring**; acceptance QA of the Six Tigers or military promotion must be performed on a legally reached qualifying state and counter-tested on disqualifying states. A sample QA jump fixture is not a credible merit history for award testing. Verify fates/date, true faction authority, state persistence, refusal, independent offices, public discovery and subsequent NPC use of the title.

**Evidence:** *Hou Hanshu* `百官志一`: https://ctext.org/wiki.pl?chapter=963008&if=gb ; Rafe de Crespigny, *Later Han Military Organisation* (2001): https://dspace-test.anu.edu.au/items/85ef1a55-57f2-4e5d-8e95-cce37e73f7fb ; novel Five Tigers summary: https://kongming.net/encyclopedia/groups/Five_Tiger_Generals_of_Shu . **Six Tigers is an explicitly alternate-history game extension, not historical evidence.**
