### Household and plural-partner planning (conditional)

### Plural-Partner Household / Harem Blueprint v0.1 — PLANNING ONLY (9 Nov 2026)
**Authority:** User-approved optional multi-partner/harem potential; Master GDD v1.11 §6D. This records **design requirements**, not implemented relationships or an asserted Han legal rule. Keep all cast identities and unearned outcomes hidden from the player.

#### Relationship and household contract
- Model one NPC per stable character ID; don't duplicate a woman as a separate 'concubine NPC' on entering the household.
- Each adult candidate has individual commitment conditions, existing exclusive promises, historical or original family obligations, awareness of other partners when relevant, boundary/refusal/exit conditions, residence, social or legal household role, independent personal arc, and continuing relationship memory.
- **No predetermined harem members.** Historical candidate eligibility must be validated against the novel, chronology, life status, location, family ties, and world divergence. Distinguish marriage/concubinage/unofficial relationships without modernizing or trivializing period power relations. No coercion, manipulation of dependents, or assumption of automatic consent.
- Potential outcomes include sincere shared domestic happiness, friendship among partners, friction, jealousy, negotiation, separate households, negotiated separation, kinship networks, and inheritance disputes. Use scene cause and NPC agency; do not require all outcomes in every playthrough.
- Household expenses and responsibilities link to the economic asset registry, not magic money. If children exist, track parentage, growth, responsibilities, kinship and succession only when authored and plausible.

#### Record schema for authored branches
`household_story_id`, `protagonist_world_date`, `scene_id`, `npc_id`, `npc_adult_status`, `mutual_consent_and_boundaries`, `existing_commitments`, `relationship_stage`, `known_partner_network`, `public_or_private_status`, `legally_plausible_role_and_source`, `residence_id`, `personal_and_household_resources`, `household_member_knowledge`, `children_if_any`, `individual_ambitions`, `relationship_memory_flags`, `approval_or_refusal_or_exit_triggers`, `political_and_family_consequences`, `downstream_scene_ids`, `epilogue_conditional_tags`, `source_verification`, `implementation_status`.

#### Four-Act opportunity and continuity matrix
| Act / chapter window | Narrative possibility, never mandatory | Gate / consequence to preserve |
|---|---|---|
| I / Chapters I–III (190–192) | First emotional bonds, adult flirtation, promises, observation of others' families; no free household | Protagonist social standing, adult consent, mutual interest, letters and distances |
| II / Chapters IV–VI (193–199) | Relationship commitments, marriage or other period-grounded ties when earned, household foundation if feasible | Money/residence, duties, NPC independent wishes, recorded promises and social obligations |
| III / Chapters VII–IX (200–213) | Long-term multiple relationship route where causally possible; domestic happiness and complex family dynamics | War absence, care, trust, children aging if any, information, property management, faction politics |
| IV / Chapters X–XII (214–234+) | Household continuity or change, mature love, family and political consequences, legacy and ending variants | Survivor/location logic, historical divergence, responsibility, succession, mortality and epilogue conditions |

#### Testing and production status
- `DESIGN_APPROVED`; `ROUTE_IMPLEMENTED=NO`; `ROUTE_BROWSER_TESTED=NO`; `HTML_CHANGED=NO`.
- When Chapter Matrix is authored, add relevant relationship dependencies only to scenes that naturally support them. Reuse existing relationship and property infrastructure; no standalone harem simulation menu or relationship collection mechanic.
- For QA, test at least one valid path for each type authored (exclusive couple, agreed plural commitment, refusal or departure, no-romance), including save/import and continuity. These are future acceptance gates, not claimed passes.

### Adult social life, venues, everyday human texture

### Adult Entertainment Venue Female NPCs — APPROVED CAST ADDENDUM (9 Nov 2026; design only)
**Latest instruction:** Introduce adult female characters at plausible brothels and pleasure/entertainment houses alongside the five long-form romance candidates. Such NPCs may be important to the local social economy and the protagonist's adult life, but are **not automatic romance routes, household members, or assumed sex workers**. Verify how each type of establishment and occupation fits its city, social class, and date before using definitive historical vocabulary.

**Suggested scene-linked roles (no mandatory headcount):** singer or instrumentalist; dancer or entertainer; housekeeper or house manager; waitress/host; independent courtesan; adult worker who provides consensual companionship when appropriate; venue owner; female patron or visiting merchant. Some roles can overlap, but never presume they are interchangeable. Use named stable IDs only for NPCs with recurring narrative functions; incidental women can remain unnamed until discoverable story reasons justify a profile or image. Each meaningful NPC has her own personal motive, preferences, private life, adult age, and ability to refuse an invitation or action.

**Scenario patterns:** a private conversation with an entertainer away from the public hall; hearing soldiers' stories at an inn attached to an entertainment venue; a well-attended performance in a politically charged city; recurring acquaintance across campaigns; spending money on recreation versus saving for home or estate; an awkward misunderstanding or lighthearted night out with companions; an offer that is refused or accepted without coercion. No compulsory paid encounter, no universal sexual availability, and non-graphic intimacy only if consenting adults and the scene genuinely requires it.

**Venue record schema:** `venue_id`, `year_window`, `region_city`, `institution_claim_source_or_TO_VERIFY`, `venue_type`, `owner_or_host`, `access_by_rank_or_reputation`, `services_actually_offered`, `fees_or_gifts_if_any`, `employee_and_patron_npc_ids`, `travel_and_safety_constraints`, `player_choices_including_decline`, `NPC_consent_and_refusal`, `confidentiality_or_gossip`, `cost_from_personal_funds_only`, `later_memories`, `related_scene_ids`, `implementation_status`.

**Cast separation:** The **five romance slots R1–R5 are the exclusive current candidate budget for designed long-form romance paths**. Supporting adult-venue women may be recurring friends, rivals, informants, acquaintances, or part of optional, non-graphic consensual adult scenes without a full route. If deliberately authored into a long-form romance, such a woman must occupy one of R1–R5, not create an unplanned sixth. Respect adult status, consent, independent agency, historical setting and discovery gates.

**Implementation status:** Original design-only venue expansion remains a scene-linked proposal. Existing performer Lan is already a named romance candidate with authored encounters; this blueprint adds no other named venue cast or assets. Verify live HTML before making any broader implementation claim.

### Adult Social Life & Festivities Blueprint v0.1 — PLANNING ONLY
**Authority:** Master GDD v1.10 §6C; user requested adult parties and everyday grown-up experiences. **This originally approved blueprint did not itself add live social events in its historical revision; verify later v0.4.44 scenes for actual implementation.** These are encounter **templates**, not declared canonical party dates or compulsory chapter scenes.

#### Event palette and literary purpose
| Social event type | Story tone and opportunities | Credible access/limitations |
|---|---|---|
| Inn, wine shop, or shared camp meal | Friendship, banter, soldier gossip, relief, potential honest conversations | Local settlement or camp, provisions, class and faction discipline, no guaranteed presence of historical figures |
| Household reunion, wedding or birth celebration | Warmth, comedy, in-law relations, gifts, obligations, family dynamics | Only when kinship, timing and travel make sense; avoid forced marriage and children |
| Village or community feast | Generosity, ritual, civilians' views of war, harvest, music | Host, availability and locale must be grounded; public feast is not universally annual or accessible |
| Gentry banquet or faction reception | Subtext, deference, patronage, invitations, negotiation, covert intelligence | Rank, faction, power, seating etiquette, hosting cost, and political risk matter |
| Victory or homecoming feast | Joy, exhaustion, mourning, gratitude, mixed feelings | No celebration when supplies, losses, discipline or political status make it implausible |
| Music, dance, performers, poetry or games | Delight, flirting, embarrassment, rivalry, personal tastes | Performers have their own social roles and agency; specific customs need source verification |
| Optional drinking, wagering or adult venue | Temptation, friendship, private emotion, awkwardness, cost and potential regret | Adult consent/age, finances, local customs, opt-out, no automatic romance or sexual service |
| Domestic evening and long-term social ties | Affection, loneliness, arguments, care, running household, children if applicable | Relationship history, household economics, war absence and independent NPC choices |

#### Four-Act social opportunity map — narrative pacing, NOT mandatory quotas
| Act / chapter window | Social texture to plan | Longitudinal payoff |
|---|---|---|
| Act I, Prologue–III (190–192) | Modest hospitality, mess meals, inns/markets, friendships, teasing, local festivities where possible | Remember early trust, debts, first invitations, class exposure and avoid premature elite status |
| Act II, IV–VI (193–199) | Patronage dinners, social rivalries, celebrations after credible accomplishments, possible adult companionship, household formation if chosen | Gifts and favour become obligations; reputations and personal commitments can cross faction lines |
| Act III, VII–IX (200–213) | Reunions after absence, household/estate hosting if earned, military receptions, family ceremonies, community festivals | Children and partners may age, managed assets bear costs, social influence expands or contracts; no empty years |
| Act IV, X–XII (214–234+) | Costly public hospitality vs private peace, generational gatherings, grief and reconciliation, friendship and legacy | Social memories, debts, estrangements and shared joy shape epilogue even for solitary protagonists |

#### Proposed event record schema
`social_event_id`, `chapter_window`, `possible_scene_ids`, `time_window`, `location_id`, `host_npc_id_or_group`, `venue_class`, `attendees_and_invitation_rules`, `arrival_and_travel_constraints`, `news_and_knowledge_delay`, `historically_supported_activities`, `cost_and_legitimate_funding`, `tone_and_pacing`, `party_npc_agency`, `relationship_state_preconditions`, `adult_age_and_consent_gates`, `player_choices_and_decline_path`, `world_flags_or_social_memory_outputs`, `consequences_to_recall`, `asset_or_household_effects`, `future_scene_dependencies`, `source_tags`, `implementation_status` (`PROPOSED`, `SOURCE_VERIFIED`, `SCENE_IMPLEMENTED`, `ROUTE_TESTED`).

#### Conditional choice → consequence examples (illustrative, spoiler-free)
- Spend hard-earned money hosting a modest reunion **versus** preserve supplies for household obligations: warmth and repaired ties versus financial resilience; no penalty if either option is credible.
- Politely decline an elite invitation **versus** attend and navigate rank/etiquette: protect privacy versus build contacts; attendance can entail gift obligations and exposure to rumours.
- Help an embarrassed or intoxicated companion **versus** leave the situation to others: remember kindness, discretion, consent and later trust; never manufacture automatic romantic access from vulnerability.
- Invite a musician/performer to a celebration **versus** keep gathering private: different moods, cost and reputational risk; performer has professional identity and freedom to refuse.
- Choose a night with family/friends instead of an additional politically useful engagement: relationships can deepen without an immediately quantifiable advantage; emotional satisfaction itself counts as a valid payoff.

#### Historical evidence and safeguards
- `CONTEXT_SOURCE`: Asian Art Museum, Han musical celebration and banquet wine service, https://exhibitions.asianart.org/exhibitions/tomb-treasures-new-discoveries-from-chinas-han-dynasty/.
- `CONTEXT_SOURCE`: Historical/archaeological review of Qin–Han music, acrobatics and banquet performances, *Heritage Science* (2026), https://www.nature.com/articles/s40494-026-02555-y.
- `CONTEXT_SOURCE`: Han alcohol production/sale and household roles, Hajni Elias (2020), *Early China*, https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/early-china/article/abs/womens-role-in-the-production-and-sale-of-alcohol-in-han-china-as-reflected-in-tomb-art-from-sichuan/78B2AA7513AA31F391FDFA3C2D41EDBF.
- `CONTEXT_SOURCE`: *Donghan Huiyao* on Han public feasts, https://ctext.org/wiki.pl?chapter=485979&if=en&remap=gb.
- `TO_VERIFY_IN_SCENE`: Each proposed celebration must be adapted to class, region, novel chronology, local political control, participant age, rank, safety and realistic news/travel. Archaeology of another Han region/century is not proof the custom was identical everywhere in 190–234 CE. Avoid modern festival names and modern commercial nightlife.
- `NON_GRAPHIC_ADULT_BOUNDARY`: Social drinking or an adult entertainment venue never implies consent to sex. Romantic and physical intimacy can be strong, warm, sensual and emotionally clear, but sexual acts are not described graphically. Preserve fully adult participants and genuine alternatives to participation.
- **Production gate:** keep this blueprint under World Bible, no new active source files, no HTML/social-stat changes. Test temporal consistency, access, funding ownership, independent NPC agency, downstream memory, and save validity when authored.

### Friendship blueprint and independence

### Companions & Lifelong Friendship Blueprint v0.1 — APPROVED DIRECTION, AUTHORING ONLY (9 Nov 2026)
**Authority:** User requests the ability to build genuine **sahabat** bonds; Master GDD v1.15 §6F. Existing `Friends`/relationship states are implementation scaffolding, **not evidence of authored close-friendship arcs**. This addendum defines production treatment, not pre-assignments or new scene code.

#### Cast and arc budget
- **3–4 major friendship arc opportunities over 12 chapters** are an editorial target, not a literal guaranteed companion count or demand for wholly new characters. Peripheral friends and acquaintances arise scene by scene. One viable opportunity should begin during Act I for plausible player routes.
- Reuse stable IDs from registered original NPCs, and historical characters where a source-checked scene could support friendship. Do not mark Liu Bei, Guan Yu, Zhang Fei, Shen Lian, Han Sheng, Xu Wen, Qin Rui or Deng Yu as an unconditional **best friend** until actual scene and player-state review. Do not overwrite historical-character duties, politics or personality to make them protagonist's pet followers.
- Female platonic friends, relatives, drinking friends, colleagues, rivals turned comrades and cross-faction friendships are valid; none counts toward the **5 romance route budget** merely because of affection.

#### Story arc templates (motifs, not fixed NPC assignments)
| Motif | What makes it memorable | What the world may change |
|---|---|---|
| **Comrade in hardship** | Shared guard duty, wounded soldiers, frightening nights, jokes about bad rations, protecting each other honestly | Rank change, injury, reassignment, death, divided loyalties |
| **Candid confidant** | Private counsel, emotional openness, celebrations, criticism that can sting, trust with secrets | Breached confidence, distance, conflicting personal duties, earned reconciliation |
| **Friendly rival / equal** | Competitive skill, teasing, taking one another seriously, meaningful respect | Different faction, career choices, policy disagreements, public rivalry |
| **Civilian / chosen-family friend** | Feasts, kinship celebrations, ordinary chores, support through injury or grief | Migration, wealth changes, household commitments, estate/crisis pressures |

#### Chapter / Act continuity requirements
| Act | Emotional beats to author when earned | Continuity gate |
|---|---|---|
| **I (190–192)** | Initial encounter, shared task or humor, memorable private exchange, trust or friction | Same location/year, NPC actually discovered; no instant lifelong bond |
| **II (193–199)** | Repeated initiative, letters or reunions, solidarity amid failures, conflict or forgiveness | Independent loyalties, dated promises, moves and credible travel |
| **III (200–213)** | Long absence and return, friendship amid major political/military change, joy or loss, sincere advice | Lives/status, factions, family changes, knowledge channels, aging |
| **IV (214–234+)** | Growing old together if alive, jokes remembered, private reconciliation, shared grief, meaningful farewell or legacy | Life/death and route-gated presence, remembered history, no mandatory tragic friendship ending |

#### Stable source/scene linking fields
`friendship_arc_id`, `npc_id`, `origin_original_or_novel`, `first_meeting_year`, `first_scene_id`, `mutual_context`, `initial_observed_traits`, `shared_experiences_by_scene`, `trust_memory_and_promises`, `friend_initiative_scenes`, `reciprocal_support_conditions`, `disagreement_or_estrangement_conditions`, `location_and_faction_by_year`, `knowledge_sources`, `independent_goals_and_household`, `life_status`, `reunion_scenes`, `late_payoff_scenes`, `conditional_epilogue_tags`, `art_reference`, `actual_implementation_status`, `route_qa_status`.

#### Scene-authoring and testing gates
1. Do not require every player to befriend every major character. Friends are gained through behavior and mutual affinity, not selected from an opening companion menu.
2. At least one designed Act I close-friendship possibility should remain narratively reachable in ordinary routes without forcing perpetual allegiance; later life permits new friends. No romance unlock is implied by friendship.
3. Give positive friendship scenes their own narrative payoff (laughter, companionship, being understood); do not reduce every social event to military buffs or plot utility.
4. On future implementation, verify save/load, old-state compatibility, knowledge/discovery, age/time and faction continuity, friend death or refusal, late callbacks, and epilogue references on **valid traversals**. As of v0.4.8 these checks remain `NOT_TESTED` for this new content.

### Economic property / estate possibility (never automatic)

### Economic Property & Estate Blueprint v0.1 — PLANNING ONLY (LoI reference)
**Authority:** User approved *Lords of Infinity*-style asset management **as inspiration**; Master GDD v1.10 §8A governs implementation and scope. **Nothing below asserts optional estate management exists in active HTML v0.4.44.** This section concerns **economic assets**, distinct from WebP portrait/illustration assets.

#### Economic asset record (add only when relevant to a scene)
`asset_id`, `kind` (`PERSONAL_ITEM`, `HORSE`, `CASH`, `RESIDENCE`, `LAND`, `WORKSHOP`, `TRADE_STAKE`, `GRAIN_STORE`, `PUBLIC_ASSIGNMENT`, other documented class), `display_name`, `source_of_title`, `legitimate_owner` (`PERSONAL`, `FAMILY`, `MANAGED_FOR_OTHER`, `OFFICE_FACTION`, `DISPUTED`), `holder_or_manager_id`, `location_id`, `acquisition_year_and_scene`, `acquisition_condition`, `value_or_price_if_grounded`, `income_source`, `upkeep`, `creditor_or_pledge`, `dependents_and_stakeholders`, `condition`, `risk_exposure`, `transfer_or_loss_conditions`, `affected_npc_memory`, `related_scene_ids`, `historical_context_source`, `implementation_status`.

#### Estate/household state (conditional, not global player stats)
- `property_id`, `asset_ids`, `manager_npc_id`, `households_or_tenants_if_story_verified`, `economic_capacity` (qualitative), `household_prosperity` (qualitative), `dependent_contentment` (qualitative), `public_standing` (qualitative), `liquidity`, `recurring_inflows`, `recurring_outflows`, `debt_terms_and_maturity`, `projects_underway`, `manager_discretion_and_orders`, `last_contact_date`, `nearby_faction_control`, `war_and_weather_risks`, `prior_decisions_and_promises`, `scene_dependency_ids`, `status` (`PROPOSED`, `SOURCE_REFERENCED`, `IMPLEMENTED`, `RUNTIME_VERIFIED`).
- When an estate does not belong to the player, **do not create phantom income or a mandatory property menu**. Office granaries, levy accounts, military supplies, and official residences are assigned resources, not personal wealth.

#### Illustrative authored choice → lasting consequence patterns (NO new scenes implemented)
| Story dilemma | Plausible immediate trade-off | Later payoff to remember |
|---|---|---|
| Repair an irrigation channel vs retain travel/army funds | Upfront resources and labor versus resilience of harvest | Future grain production and household goodwill, or transport/credit constraints |
| Ease or press tenant obligations after a poor harvest | Less immediate income versus unrest and livelihood risk | More trust/productivity later, or grievance, displacement and vulnerability to rivals |
| Fund grain reserves or expand trade venture | Security against shortage versus speculative return and supply risk | Refugee support or famine buffer; debt/exposure when routes are disrupted |
| Leave family steward in charge while campaigning | Less daily control versus ability to pursue military/political story | Steward loyalty/competence, delayed messages, surprises and remembered instructions |
| Accept politically conditional grant or creditor help | Immediate property/cash versus loyalty/debt obligations | Legitimacy disputes, rival claims, faction pressure or social costs |

#### Chapter opportunity windows (not mandatory missions)
| Period | Economic/story opportunity | Continuity constraints |
|---|---|---|
| Chapters I–III (190–192) | Savings, equipment, wages/awards, lodgings, immediate obligations | Opening unaffiliated soldier **does not begin with a free barony or estate** |
| Chapters IV–VI (193–199) | Conditional land/premises/trade stake, acquisition, household support, debt or patron obligations | Depends on career, means, kinship, credibility, locality, period law |
| Chapters VII–IX (200–213) | Estate projects or delegated management **if property obtained**; otherwise personal/business/official resource responsibility | Multi-year time skips, war damage, manager communication and ownership distinctions |
| Chapters X–XII (214–234+) | Loss, protection, growth, transfer and legacy; political position may grant public stewardship without private title | Creditor and dependent outcomes, succession, conflict, retirement, epilogues |

#### Implementation and validation queue
1. Finish World & Narrative Blueprint before HTML changes; map feasible asset decisions to preexisting relevant chapter beats and careers (do not force 12 estate turns).
2. Research Han-era economic reality for each specific region and year: land rights, tenants, tax/grain regimes, monetary units, debt and social obligations. *Lords of Infinity* sources justify only **inspiration**, never period facts.
3. Establish minimal economic state and continuity rules before coding costs; balance debt/expenses and ensure an estate-free playthrough is coherent.
4. Write authored narrative, not management grids: pressure from relatives, human tenant stories, humor, hardship, negotiations, politics, weather and wartime logistics. Check ripple effects and eventual epilogues.
5. Targeted save/state/ownership QA when implemented; avoid claiming the active v0.4.44 build already has these optional estate features.

## WB-07 — HISTORICAL EVENT MAP AND RESEARCH GATES

Use the approved 190–234+ milestones as *anchors*, not mandatory battlefield appearances. Each scene checks chronology, geography, source-vs-novel disagreement, office, factions, message latency, actual player presence, and divergence prerequisites. Xu (imperial city) and Xu Province are different places. Xiapi news in early 198 must not be narrated as if the campaign's outcome is already known. Guandu in Chapter VII requires 200 CE date checks, delayed news, and no all-knowing retrospective narration.

### Historical Event Map v1.0 — APPROVED TIMELINE FRAMEWORK / RESEARCH STARTER
**Decision date:** 9 November 2026. **Authority:** Approved Option C (Historical Milestones + Flexible Lifetime). **Exposure:** Internal development document; chat and playable UI remain discovery-gated. **Implementation:** Event map planning only; v0.4.44 world-year entry migration has been implemented separately (WB-01/WB-02). These are reference events from the literary/historical timeline, *not mandatory events in every player path*.

#### Sources, tags and chronology confidence
- `NOVEL_REF`: Kongming's Archives, *Romance of the Three Kingdoms (Sanguo yanyi) Chronology*: https://kongming.net/novel/chronology/ — cross-check novel-ordered years, identities, sequence and literary embellishments against the relevant original novel chapters before final publication. An online chronology is a **secondary guide**, not the verbatim novel.
- `HISTORY_CONTEXT`: historical/political-military chronology for comparison: https://swordandstate.com/en/history/ and https://shijisanguo.com/lang/en/ . Date disagreements or novel inventions should be recorded explicitly and should **not** silently overwrite novel characterization.
- `NOVEL_VS_HISTORY`: contextual overview on novel embellishments, e.g. https://sinochronicle.com/en/topics/history-vs-romance/ . Do not present a literary invention as a documented historical occurrence.
- `OPTIONAL_WORLD_ANCHOR`: an event can exist offscreen, become news after a plausible delay, be indirectly encountered, or never happen because a causal divergence changed the world.
- `TO_VALIDATE_IN_SCENE`: exact chapter scene, NPC year/age/office, location, travel, reachability, player knowledge, and world divergence are **not yet QA-approved**.

| Chapter | Planning window | NOVEL_REF / HISTORY_CONTEXT event anchors (historical baseline, **conditional** on world state) | World-building and continuity requirements | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Prologue | 190 | Anti-Dong Zhuo coalition and disorder of the Han court; aftermath of the court's westward move | Establish local knowledge and the protagonist's uninvolved perspective; ordinary people may know only rumors | BLUEPRINT / SCENE VALIDATION NEEDED |
| I | 190 | Coalition against Dong Zhuo; unrest near Luoyang/Chenliu and fragmentation of authority | Chenliu departure, Liu Bei circle's plausible location; period titles and no global omniscience | BLUEPRINT / SCENE VALIDATION NEEDED |
| II | 190–191 | Coalition strains and collapses; rivalry among the eastern lords; Yuan Shao–Gongsun Zan and Sun Jian–Liu Biao rivalry start/expand | Preserve existing Chapter I → II choices, travel and duration; no claim player attends all events | BLUEPRINT / SCENE VALIDATION NEEDED |
| III | 191–192 | Yuan Shao–Gongsun Zan contest; political upheaval surrounding Dong Zhuo's downfall (192); Cao Cao consolidating in Yan | `ch2_35` → `ch03_01` immediate-morning handoff must not conceal an unexplained year jump; establish location and faction news latency | BLUEPRINT / SCENE VALIDATION NEEDED |
| IV | 193–195 | Xu Province crisis and Liu Bei's changing regional position; Cao Cao's Yan Province struggles; Sun Ce's early Jiangdong advances; imperial flight | Civilian displacement, changing control/obligations and moving factions; keep character ages and regional politics coherent | BLUEPRINT / SCENE VALIDATION NEEDED |
| V | 196–197 | Emperor Xian and Cao Cao's court at Xu (196); Yuan Shu's imperial claim (197); Cao Cao–Zhang Xiu / Wancheng conflict | Legal legitimacy, appointments, court power and distance between court and field commands | BLUEPRINT / SCENE VALIDATION NEEDED |
| VI | 198–199 | Xiapi/Lü Bu's defeat (historical 198; novel chronology may place execution in 199); Yuan Shu's collapse; Gongsun Zan's downfall | **v0.4.44 starts Chapter VI at 198:** verify scene-level milestones before coding battle aftermath; reflect novel/history date differences | CLOCK_ALIGNED_V044 / SCENE_SOURCE_REVIEW_NEEDED |
| VII | 200–207 | Guandu campaign (200); Sun Ce/Sun Quan succession (200); Yuan family conflict and northern consolidation; Liu Bei's Jingzhou period; the approach of Zhuge Liang's early involvement | Long interval needs multiple social/military/political arcs, not empty time jump; relationships, household, offices and NPC age evolve | BLUEPRINT / LONG ARC NEEDED |
| VIII | 208–209 | Jingzhou crisis, Changban, Red Cliffs (208); southern commandery struggles and post-war control (208–209) | Army movement, climate, supply, alliances and rumor delay; novel heroics must follow characterization and possible divergence | BLUEPRINT / SCENE VALIDATION NEEDED |
| IX | 210–213 | Jingzhou control disputes and alliances; western outreach and the Yi Province campaign beginning (211–213); competing western/eastern war objectives | Alliances, social/household development, where each NPC actually serves, political legitimacy and travel times | BLUEPRINT / SCENE VALIDATION NEEDED |
| X | 214–219 | Yi Province control / Chengdu (214); Hanzhong campaigns and political claims; Jingzhou–Fancheng crisis (219) | Multi-year arcs and layered consequences, faction legitimacy, command distance; novel may place some aftermath in 220 | BLUEPRINT / LONG ARC NEEDED |
| XI | 220–223 | Han–Wei succession (220); Shu founding (221); conflict around Yiling (221–222); political and personal changes by 223 | **v0.4.44 enters Chapter XII in 224:** verify late XI bridges/alternate-world actor survival and no forced canonical outcomes | CLOCK_ALIGNED_V044 / SCENE_CONTINUITY_NEEDED |
| XII | 224–234+ | Later Wei/Wu/Shu politics; Nanzhong (225), Northern Expeditions (228 onward), Wu's imperial proclamation (229), Wuzhang (234) where unaffected by divergence | Resolution can arrive at different ages and dates; epilogue completes a **whole lifetime** without compulsory survival to or death in 234 | CLOCK_ALIGNED_V044 / SCENE_SOURCE_REVIEW_NEEDED |
| Epilogues | Flexible | World state follows actual player-caused outcomes, with canonical 234+ references only if plausible | Relational/household/political aftermath and personal legacy, not a mandated world history summary | APPROVED RULE; ROUTES NOT QA-VERIFIED |

#### Event-to-scene contract (minimum per event actually used)
Use these World Bible record fields, not a new standalone active file:
- `event_id` e.g. `HIST_0200_GUANDU`, `novel_source_locator`, `history_context_locator`, `year_window`, `location_ids`, `faction_ids`, `npc_ids`, `scene_ids` (specific, once mapped).
- `baseline_event_state`: `NOT_YET`, `ONGOING`, `RESOLVED`, `AVERTED`, `ALTERED`; event states depend on *causal* preconditions, not chapter number alone.
- `player_direct_presence`: `ONSCREEN`, `INDIRECT`, `OFFSCREEN`, `NOT_RELEVANT`; historical importance does not imply player attends the event.
- `npc_known_facts`, `protagonist_known_facts`, `rumor_delivery_delay`, `travel_time_constraints`, `authority_and_rank_at_date`, `household_and_commitment_impacts`.
- `divergence_causes`, `new_outcomes`, `downstream_dependents`, `last_verified_source`, `verification_status` (`TO_VERIFY`, `NOVEL_REF_CHECKED`, `HISTORY_CROSSCHECKED`, `RUNTIME_VERIFIED`).

### v0.4.55 Prelude historical-source gate (208)
- Review sources independently for historical baseline: Liu Biao death/Liu Cong surrender, Liu Bei's move from Jing, Changban flight, Zhang Fei's rearguard and Zhao Yun's rescue of Liu Bei's household, Guan Yu's boat junction at Hanjin, Liu Qi and his Jiangxia forces; in historical sequence Xu Shu leaves to join his captive mother, while Zhuge Liang proceeds with diplomacy. [*Records of the Three Kingdoms*, Liu Bei](https://sanguozhi.app/en/chapter/32), [Cambridge History of China—Rafe de Crespigny](https://api.pageplace.de/preview/DT0400.9781108660464_A45555048/preview-9781108660464_A45555048.pdf), [Rafe de Crespigny Red Cliffs 208 chapter](https://brill.com/previewpdf/book/9789004188303/Bej.9789004185227.i-554_008.xml).
- Original story invention: the protagonist's troop movement, role in Changban, Xu Shu's invented discussion, the councils with Liu Qi, particular documents, tactical successes, humor and military compact. None is attested verbatim. Avoid confusing novel elaborations with historical biographies; keep player divergence causally earned, not guaranteed.

### v0.4.51 Historical source-gate note
- Source matches 208 Zhuge Liang / Lu Su Liu Bei–Jiangdong diplomatic coordination (Wu Shu Lu Su biography, Shu Shu Liu Bei biography and ROTK chapter 43). Other details are creative invention. In-world village/river bank labels are intentionally nonspecific; do not falsely call this player's river-account council a historical event.
- **v0.4.51 ARCHIVED RESTRICTION, SUPERSEDED:** Earlier chapter mechanics restricted the protagonist to a ferry/requisition register and prohibited player-created battle victory. The 10 Nov user correction **overrides this limitation**. In v0.4.52 the protagonist may command a force and personally alter battle operations; the larger alternate-world war outcomes still require authored faction and later-chapter recomputation. News of people in another location must still obey physical travel and message delay.

### Change risk rules

- **FAST:** local text/format; verify markup/JS syntax, unchanged scene IDs and existing choice maps, one legally reachable local path; preserve save.
- **STANDARD:** new NPC, flags, romance/friendship callbacks, choices, artwork; verify prior/after scenes, `open|respect|distance`, no-meeting old save, source-legal fixture, discovery timing and state validity. A flag applied on a choice **exit** cannot be used to determine dialogue already displayed in that same scene.
- **DEEP:** clock/age, actual save version/structure, engine, endpoint arbitration; compare broad branches, migrated saves, real UI, responsiveness and persistent storage where environment permits. Explicitly distinguish mock-origin test from native file-browser roundtrip.
- **At integrated milestones:** full route and eight-ending regression with valid imported fixtures, historical actor/geography checks, adult consent, conditional estate and family; no claim of completion from missing branches or static link checks.

### Current to-do queue (do not revive obsolete issues)

1. **Chapter VII 200–207**: year-by-year first pass now implemented in v0.4.45; expand depth, consequential later callbacks, branch-specific geography and historical NPC source gates. Do not replace scenes with retrospectives.
2. **Chapter VI 198–199**: first late-198/199 bridge and consequences implemented in v0.4.46; continue novel-scale polishing, scene-specific source verification and differentiated aftermath of 2 new choice groups.
3. **Historical People expansion**: source-verifiable scene-linked actors with bounded knowledge, real meeting gates and distinct character voices; do not pre-research entire 112-person target.
4. **Six romance journeys and friendships:** v0.4.49–0.4.50 provide private-scene opportunities for six different women but **do not complete their lifelong routes**. Continue independent lives, conflicts and reconciliations, earned historical outcomes, friendship depth, and consequential eight-ending callbacks, especially after 220.
5. **Specific later timeline**: v0.4.73 has *conditional verified/uncertain report language* for 219–223 and player-owned river obligations through 234. Still **OPEN**: independently modeled actor survival, titles, office, time-lag and succession under radically divergent outcomes, and distinct living NPC actions outside inherited courier records; no forced spouse/children/estate.
6. **QA debt**: native file-origin persistence/download, multi-route late-game acceptance, portraits for uncovered historical actors, text-level editing, any exact birthdays only if later approved.

### Minimal active update discipline

For each meaningful batch, update only: (a) **WB-01** build SHA/status + issues; (b) touched NPCs in WB-03/04; (c) corresponding chapter card in WB-05 or event record in WB-07; (d) relevant QA evidence in WB-08. Do **not** append another full 'LATEST v0.4.xx' block at the top or repeat the same QA story in three sections. Keep one chronological checkpoint line in WB-09; full receipts live only in archive when no longer current.
